- **What a tier means.** Analytics counted each participant at the tier the ladder or a league lookup holds them at *now*, so a promotion moved all of a player's old games and no total could be added to. Now each participant of a solo or flex match gets a row in `match_tiers` (V0016) in the transaction that archives the match, at ADR-105's tier as it is then: the newer of `ladder_entries` and `player_ranks` for the match's platform (its id's prefix) and queue, else `UNKNOWN`. `INSERT OR IGNORE`: a re-archive never restamps. Other queues write nothing. The table is apart from `match_facts`, so facts stay a pure derivation of the body and `facts:reextract` doesn't touch it.
- **Late stamps (owner question 4).** A rank the ladder (`store::write_page`) or a league lookup (`ranks::record`) returns sets that player's `UNKNOWN` rows on the same platform and queue to it, in the same write transaction, for matches that ended within `TIER_LATE_STAMP_DAYS` (14; 0 turns it off). The plan says "started"; the archive holds `game_end_ms`, and a game's length is immaterial against a 14-day window. A known tier is never changed. The next rebuild reads the new tier.
- **The rebuild reads the stamp.** `ladder_facts()` joins `match_tiers` by `(match_id, puuid)` with its key scope, platform and queue, in place of the two rank tables. A fact with no stamp doesn't count. `rebuild_matchups` is unchanged: matchups have no tier until FLT-01, and every ranked match with facts is stamped, so its rows are the same either way. FLT-01 joins it there.
- **Backfill: a job, run inline by every rebuild.** `tiers:backfill` stamps ranked matches with facts and no stamps, 500 at a time, found on a reader and written in one short transaction each, paced like `facts:reextract`. Its tier is ADR-105's from today's tables (the plan said `ladder_entries` alone, which predates ADR-105). That is exactly what every rebuild before V0016 read, so the first rebuild after the upgrade gives the same rows. It is queued at boot when anything is unstamped, and `aggregate:analytics` runs it before rebuilding (as it does `builds:extract`), so no rebuild ever reads a half-stamped archive. Once everything is stamped, the check is one walk of `matches`' key on a reader. Not done in the migration (as V0015 did) so a large archive doesn't hold up boot.
- **The `/dev` reset** empties `match_tiers`.
- **Tests:** `src/archive/tiers/tests.rs` (a solo match with 3 placed players writes 10 rows, those 3 at their tiers, a newer lookup winning, the rest `UNKNOWN`; a re-archive after a promotion keeps the first stamp; an ARAM match writes none; `write_derived` leaves the stamps alone; a later lookup places an `UNKNOWN` row inside the window and not outside, never changes a known tier, and another queue's or platform's rank doesn't apply; a ladder page does the same; the backfill finds and stamps unstamped matches); `src/archive/analytics/tests.rs` (every fixture, rebuilt after stamping, gives byte-identical rows; the facts plan reaches the stamp by key); `tests/analytics.rs` (a promoted player's old games stay in the old tier after a second rebuild, the new ones count in the new tier).

## ADR-128 — The API serves every row; a games floor is the caller's (2026-10-10)
Accepted (owner request 2026-10-10, task SITE-11). Supersedes the `AGGREGATE_MIN_GAMES` default of ADR-056's routes; amends ADR-121 (builds' `minGames`).
- **Why.** With the default floor of 10, an item, rune page, build or champion seen in fewer than 10 games of a slice was dropped, so a build that had been played once didn't show. Every row already carries `games`, so the caller can judge the sample. The owner's rule: the API shows all the data it has, and a floor is set by the consuming app, or by a public route that filters, added later.
- **`minGames` defaults to 0** on `/v1/lol/analytics/champions`, `/champions/{championId}` (every section) and `/champions/{championId}/builds`. `/matchups` already did. A caller's `minGames` works as before.
- **`AGGREGATE_MIN_GAMES` is removed**, not defaulted to 0: a server-wide floor is the thing being removed. A deployment that still sets it boots and ignores it, as with any variable the config doesn't read.
- **ETags** carry the resolved `minGames`, so a client's old validator (taken under 10) doesn't match the new, longer responses.
- **Tests:** `tests/analytics.rs` (`without_min_games_every_row_is_served_even_a_one_game_one`: with `AGGREGATE_MIN_GAMES=10` set, one-game rows on the list, every detail section and builds; `minGames=2` still empties them). The other analytics tests no longer set the variable.


## ADR-129 — Per-participant analytics rows carry their player's tier and side (2026-10-10)
Accepted (owner request 2026-10-10, task FLT-01). design/04 §Schema; amends ADR-120 and ADR-121 (their tables gain two key columns), builds on ADR-127.
- **Why.** FLT-02 filters the champion page's builds and matchups by tier and side, and FLT-03 by tier range. Only `champion_stats`, `champion_bans` and `analytics_slices` had a tier; matchups and every build table had none, and nothing had a side.
- **Keys.** `champion_matchups`, `champion_items`, `champion_runes`, `champion_spells`, `champion_builds` and `champion_build_parts` gain `tier` and `side`; `champion_stats` gains `side`. A row's tier is its own player's stamp (`match_tiers`, ADR-127), and its side is `blue` for team 100 and `red` for team 200 (design/10 §Scoreboard layout). A matchup's tier and side are `champion_id`'s player's; the opponent may be in another tier. Per-match tables (`analytics_slices`, `champion_bans`, the V0015 totals) get no side. Facts of any other team id don't count, and Summoner's Rift has no others.
- **Last in the key.** Both columns go after the ones the reads filter on, so every read keeps its key prefix and query plan; the DEV-26/THR-02/BLD-02 plan tests pass unchanged, and the matchups plan reaches the new stamp join by `(match_id, puuid)`.
- **Reads unchanged.** Rows are disjoint across tiers and sides, so summing is exact. `Read::facet`, `builds`, `build_totals`, `build_parts`, `top_role` and `patches` already summed every row that matched; `stats` groups by champion (and tier when asked) and sums the sides. `matches_picked` sums exactly too: a champion is on one team per match. The route snapshots in `tests/analytics.rs` are byte-identical.
- **Matchups join the stamp.** `MATCHUPS` joins `match_tiers` for the row's player after the lane's head count, which still counts every fact. A fact with no stamp has no row, as in every other step; every ranked match with facts is stamped before a rebuild (ADR-127), so no row is lost.
- **Old patches: one rebuild over every patch, not a backfill in the migration.** A rebuild replaces only the newest `AGGREGATE_PATCH_LIMIT` patches, so older rows have to come from somewhere. Re-deriving them in V0017's SQL, as V0015 did, would need every rebuild query copied into the migration, and it would read `match_tiers`, which can still be empty when V0017 runs (`tiers:backfill` runs after boot). V0017 copies each row as it is, with tier and side `''` ("not split yet"); since reads sum over both, every route answers as before. It lists every ladder that had rows in a new `analytics_unsplit`. `aggregate:analytics` reads that list first: a listed ladder is rebuilt over every patch, whatever the limit, and taken off the list once all three steps succeed. A failed step leaves it listed, so the next run starts over. Boot queues that rebuild for each listed ladder of the current key scope (`split_if_needed`), deduplicated like any other. Nothing ever deletes matches outside the `/dev` reset, so a full rebuild reproduces every patch. The `/dev` reset empties the list with the rest.
- **Migration time.** The plan asked for a timing against a copy of the dev VM database; this container can't reach the VM. On a synthetic database with 4.6 million analytics rows (528 MB, 2 million of them build parts), V0017 took 12 s here. The cost is a copy of each table, linear in its rows. The full rebuild afterwards costs what an `AGGREGATE_PATCH_LIMIT=0` rebuild costs, which is the dev VM's setting already.
- **Tests:** `src/archive/analytics/tests.rs` (a hand-computed match puts champion 1 on both sides and gives champion 2's lane rows in two tiers; per-tier and per-side rows of stats, matchups, items, runes, spells, builds and a build part; the fixture's existing expectations hold when summed over tier and side; the matchups plan reaches the stamp by key); `src/db/tests.rs` (V0017 keeps every row with tier and side `''`, `champion_stats` its tier, and lists both ladders); `tests/analytics.rs` (rows left unsplit give the same five route documents, boot queues one rebuild, a one-patch rebuild splits an older patch's rows and empties the list, and later rebuilds keep to the limit again).

## ADR-130 — Every analytics route filters by tier and side (2026-10-10)
Accepted (owner request 2026-10-10, task FLT-02). design/11 §Page map; builds on ADR-129, amends ADR-123's ETag shape marker.
- **Why.** FLT-01 keyed every per-participant row by its player's tier and side. This exposes both, so the champion page can show builds and matchups for one rank and one side.
- **`tier` on every champion route.** `GET …/champions/{id}/matchups` and `…/builds` take it, and the detail route's matchups, items, runes and spells now honour it; all of these ignored it before. A matchup's tier is the champion's player's, not the opponent's. The validation is the champions route's: Riot's tiers plus `UNKNOWN`.
- **`side` on every analytics route** (`champions`, the detail, `matchups`, `builds`, `patches`): `blue` or `red`, from team 100 or 200. It filters the per-participant tables only. A pick rate on one side is that side's picks over every match in the slice, so blue plus red is the both-sides rate. Ban rates and the match denominators (`analytics_slices`, `champion_bans`, the V0015 totals) have no side and don't change. Omitted, both sides are summed, as before. `patches` takes `side` but not `tier`, as the plan says. Its games are a picker's labels, and the champion page's patch list stays the champion's every game.
- **Any other side is a 400 in the routes' usual shape.** The plan names `INVALID_PARAM`, but every query value on these routes, `tier` and `remakes` included, is a 400 with code `VALIDATION` and the message `querystring/side must be equal to one of the allowed values`. The plan's own wording, "in the route's usual shape", wins over the code it names.
- **Bodies name the slice.** Every body has `side` (`null` when none was asked for), and the matchups and builds bodies gain `tier`, as the champions and detail bodies already had.
- **ETags.** `side` is part of every route's ETag. The shape marker becomes `tier-side` and now also goes into the matchups and builds ETags. The same query without `tier` or `side` returns a body with one more field, and the same query with `tier` returns different rows, so no validator issued before this can match.
- **The side filter in SQL** is a constant from the `Side` enum, never the caller's text. It goes into the statement, not a bound parameter, so the reads' numbered parameters don't move.
- **Showcase.** The champion page's header has a tier picker (All tiers, each tier, Unknown) and a Both · Blue · Red toggle. Either reloads the By tier, Builds and Matchups cards. Each card names its slice in its header. The choice is kept across champions.
- **MU-02's baselines** haven't landed, so FLT-02's (c) has nothing to change yet. MU-02 must read its baselines in the request's tier and side.
- **Tests:** `src/archive/analytics/tests.rs` (`reads_filter_by_tier_and_side`: matchups by the champion's player's tier and by side, items, set builds, build totals and parts, stats on one side with unchanged slices, `top_role` and `patches` by side, all hand-computed); `tests/analytics.rs` (`tier_and_side_slice_every_route_and_add_up_to_the_total`: a game with players alternating Master and Grandmaster; on every route Master plus Grandmaster and blue plus red each equal the total, and the slices differ; every body names its slice; `side` changes every ETag; `side=green` is a 400 on all five routes); the OpenAPI and route snapshots; `tests/dom/showcase.test.mjs` (the tier picker and the side toggle reload the three cards with `tier=MASTER` and then `&side=red`, each card names the slice, and clearing both restores the plain queries); the Chromium layout suite at three widths.

## ADR-131 — Viego's possession isn't counted as his skill points (2026-10-10)
Accepted (writeup from ninjagoldfinch.lol 2026-10-10, owner answers the same day, task BLD-05). Amends ADR-117 (`extract`'s inputs) and ADR-122 (`playerBuild`'s inputs, the skill grid).
- **Why.** When Viego possesses a champion, Riot's timeline can log the possessed champion's ranks as Viego's own `SKILL_LEVEL_UP`s with `levelUpType: NORMAL`, in the millisecond the possession swaps his inventory (a burst of his `ITEM_DESTROYED`). The definitions counted every `NORMAL` level-up, so in OC1_712417978 Viego had 15 points at level 13, with Nidalee's two R ranks as his 14th and 15th.
- **The rule.** For Viego (championId 234) only, a `SKILL_LEVEL_UP` at the same timestamp as one or more of his own `ITEM_DESTROYED` events is not counted. It is dropped before anything reads the level-ups: `skills`, `skillOrder`, and the showcase's `levelUps` and grid.
- **Only Viego, checked against the archive first.** Over the dev VM's 27,411 archived timelines (281,994 participants), Viego has 1,622 `NORMAL` level-ups sharing a millisecond with his own `ITEM_DESTROYED`; 1,522 of them are two or more level-ups in one ms, at 755 bursts with two or more items destroyed. The other 97 champions have 204 such level-ups between them, none of them a burst with an inventory swap: almost all are one point beside one item (Aphelios 26, Morgana 6, Karma 5). A blanket rule would drop those real points, so the rule names the champion.
- **What's left over `champLevel + 1` is real, so nothing is capped.** Before the rule, 1,523 participants had more than `champLevel + 1` points (309 Viego, 1,214 Aphelios); after it, 1,215. Aphelios's R ranks itself at 6, 11 and 16 and is logged as a `NORMAL` level-up on top of the point he spends on a stat at that level, so he ends at `champLevel + 3`; the owner confirmed he should display as now. The one Viego left is an Arena game (queue 1750), where levels come two at a time and his R ups at level 3 have no item destroyed beside them; Arena builds aren't displayed for now (owner), so it stays out of scope. Triple Tonic (rune 8313), R ranked at level 1 (Nidalee, Jayce, Elise, Karma) and Udyr's ranks are real too, which is why the fix keys on the possession signal and not on a cap by level, by rank totals or by R at 6/11/16.
- **The champion comes from the match, not the timeline.** The timeline names no champions. `extract(timeline, &catalog, &champions)` takes the match's puuid → championId; `builds:extract` reads it from `match_facts` (keyed by puuid, which the timeline's `info.participants` also carries) in the same read as the batch. That is the smallest change: the job already has the match id and the facts, and `extract` stays pure and panic-free. A player missing from the map is treated as any champion but Viego. `playerBuild(timeline, puuid, catalog, championId)` gets the player's `championId` from the match view, which has every player's; the helper looks nothing up.
- **`BUILDS_VERSION` 1 → 2.** `builds:extract` rewrites every `match_builds` row before the next recompute, which rebuilds the set builds' `skill_order` parts from them. With `AGGREGATE_PATCH_LIMIT` above 0, patches older than the limit keep their aggregates until a full rebuild, as after any version bump; the dev VM runs with 0.
- **The grid shows every point.** The match page's skill grid had 18 columns, so a 19th point (Udyr with Triple Tonic) was cut off. It now has a column per point, at least 18, headed "points in the order taken": with Triple Tonic the 10th point comes at level 9, so a column is not a level. `skills` keeps the first 15.
- **Not in this task:** whether Udyr's `skillOrder` should rank R with Q, W and E. The owner said yes (2026-10-10); it is a separate definition change and gets its own task.
- **Tests:** `src/archive/builds/tests.rs` (the new golden file; Viego's level-up in the ms of his `ITEM_DESTROYED` is dropped, with one item or several; the same events for Zed, or an unknown champion, are kept; Viego's level-up with no `ITEM_DESTROYED` in its ms, or beside another player's, is kept; a Triple Tonic player, Zed or Viego, keeps the level-9 point; `OC1_711969250` and its snapshot unchanged); `tests/builds.rs` (`builds:extract` applies the rule with the facts' champions); `tests/showcase.mjs` (`playerBuild` equals both golden files for every player, with each match's champions; the same Viego, non-Viego and Triple Tonic cases); `tests/dom/showcase.test.mjs` (a player with 19 points gets 19 columns headed as points, 18 otherwise; with that player made Viego the match view passes his champion and the possessed R ranks go).

## ADR-132 — Udyr's skill order ranks R with Q, W and E (2026-10-10)
Accepted (owner answer 2026-10-10 to ninjagoldfinch.lol's question in the BLD-05 writeup, task BLD-06). Amends ADR-117 and ADR-122 (the skill order's definition).
- **Why.** The skill order ranks Q, W and E because for nearly every champion R is the ultimate, taken whenever it can be. Udyr's R is one of four ordinary abilities with the same ranks as the others, and Udyr players max it first more often than not, so a three-letter order left out what they chose. The owner said Udyr's R should count as a normal skill.
- **The rule.** For Udyr (championId 77), the skill order ranks Q, W, E and R by points at the end, a tie going to the skill that got there first, a never-levelled skill last in Q W E R order: four letters, e.g. `"RWEQ"`. For everyone else nothing changes.
- **The champion is already passed in.** BLD-05 gave `extract` the match's puuid → championId and `playerBuild` the player's `championId`, so this needs no new input. A player whose champion isn't known is ranked as anyone else.
- **Checked against the dev VM's archive.** 862 Udyr players have a timeline. Under the new rule the most common orders are `RWEQ` (293), `QEWR` (228), `QWER` (72), `QERW` (64) and `RWQE` (62); taking R out of each gives the old three-letter order for all 862, so only R's place is new.
- **Udyr takes points at 19 and 20.** The writeup said levels 19–20 give no point and that Udyr can reach 19. In the archive, ranked Udyrs at level 20 with Triple Tonic have 21 points, with a point at each of levels 19 and 20 (`L19@1926 Q@1933 L20@2078 Q@2079` in OC1_706102889): six ranks to each ability is 24, more than his levels. The definitions now say so; nothing caps points, so no code changes for it.
- **`BUILDS_VERSION` 2 → 3**, so `builds:extract` rewrites `match_builds` and the next recompute rebuilds the set builds' `skill_order` parts. A `skill_order` part for Udyr becomes four letters; the builds route and the showcase show whatever letters they get. The route's `BuildSkillOrderOption` description names the four-letter form (OpenAPI snapshot).
- **Tests:** `src/archive/builds/tests.rs` (the new golden file; Udyr ranks R, a never-levelled skill last, a tie to the first to get there; the same events for Zed or an unknown champion rank three; the earlier golden files and snapshot unchanged); `tests/showcase.mjs` (`playerBuild` equals all three golden files for every player; the same cases); `tests/dom/showcase.test.mjs` (with a player made Udyr, the match view passes his champion and the card shows `Q › E › R › W`; as Nidalee, `Q › E › W`); the OpenAPI snapshot.

## ADR-133 — A bounded, lowest-priority backfill for missing timelines (2026-10-11)
Accepted (owner request and answers 2026-10-11, task TL-02). design/04 §Schema, design/06 §Job catalogue and §Priority; builds on ADR-118, leaves it unchanged.
- **Why.** Set builds and the match page's build need a timeline. On the VPS (2026-10-10), 92,602 of 92,796 ranked solo matches had none: every match of 16.16 and older, and 199 of 16.17–16.20. Viego's champion page counted 1,473 games and its builds 128.
- **Cause.** Until DEV-23 (#118, 2026-10-09) `ARCHIVE_TIMELINES` defaulted to false. A crawl copies the setting into each `archive:match` it queues as an explicit `fetchTimeline: false`, and an explicit value beats the setting when the job runs. On the VPS, 92,512 such downloads had run and stored their matches without a timeline, and 1,191,951 were still queued; turning the setting on doesn't reach them. TL-01's store hook doesn't catch them either: it queues the missing half under the match's dedupe key, which the running download holds, so nothing is queued. Only TL-01's boot catch-up finds them. Every one of the 199 recent gaps had a finished crawl download (`fetchTimeline: 0`) and a pending catch-up job; none had failed, and the log had no timeline errors.
- **Not an outage.** The VPS restarted at 18:56 UTC on 2026-10-10; its catch-up queued 92,677 timelines at priority 100 and fetched about 410 in its first 4½ minutes, about 1.5 a second over four regions. The owner chose to let it finish, about 17 hours, with the crawl waiting behind it. TL-01 is otherwise unchanged: a match stored now still queues its timeline at priority 0, and the catch-up still runs at every boot.
- **What TL-02 adds.** What the catch-up lacks: a bound, an order, the lowest priority, a memory of 404s, and progress. The crawl's 1.19M queued downloads keep storing matches without a timeline (owner: keep them one call each), and TL-02 is how those get theirs.
  - **Bound:** `TIMELINE_BACKFILL_PATCHES`, the newest N patches with ranked matches (queues 420 and 440, which set builds read). 0, the default, is off; the owner sets it on the VPS.
  - **Order:** newest patch first (patches compared as numbers, as the rebuilds do), then newest `game_end_ms`, so the builds people look at fill first. `archived_at` isn't used; it is the day of the move to the VPS for every match.
  - **Budget:** priority 40 000, a band below maintenance, so a backfill job is claimed only when nothing else is. Its fetches are ordinary bulk fetches (`FetchOptions::JOB`): `BULK_USAGE_CEILING` (80%, 95% while a region is idle) and the interactive reserve hold, the limiter yields the job while there's no room, and a typed 429's `Retry-After` freezes the scope and the job yields until it ends. The owner accepted that a busy crawl starves it.
  - **One job per region,** in its region's lane (`match.timeline`), deduped by region: each region's limit is separate, and a region with no room doesn't hold up the others.
  - **Turns, no cursor:** 25 timelines a turn, then `take_turns`. What is left is read from the archive every turn, so a restart resumes and a second run asks for nothing. A timeline that arrives another way (TL-01's catch-up, a visitor) simply drops out.
  - **404s** go in `timeline_gaps` (V0018, no foreign key, like `timelines`) and are never asked for again; Riot answers 404 for a game it no longer keeps. Other failures back off and retry as any job's; after five the job fails and the hourly tick queues it again.
  - **Builds follow:** a turn that stored timelines queues `builds:extract` (deduped, so one at a time); the recompute picks the rows up on its own schedule. No recompute is forced.
  - **Progress:** `GET /v1/admin/timelines/backfill` gives, per patch, ranked matches, with a timeline, not found and left, and per region what is left and the patch it's on; `?patches=N` previews a wider bound before turning it up. `timeline_backfill_fetched_total` and `timeline_backfill_not_found_total`, by region, count the work, and the job's activity line names the patch and match.
- **How long.** A personal key allows 100 calls per 2 minutes per region: about 0.67 a second for bulk (0.79 while idle), about 2.7 a second over four regions if the work is spread evenly. The 92k already missing are TL-01's and take about 17 hours as observed (sea, 28,342 of them, is the long pole at about 12 hours alone). TL-02 runs only when the queue is otherwise empty; on the VPS that is after the crawl's 1.19M downloads, about 5–6 days at that rate. The timelines of those 1.19M matches, if all inside the bound, take about as long again. A restart before then runs TL-01's catch-up, which queues every missing timeline at 100, ahead of the crawl.
- **Tests:** `src/jobs/timelines/tests.rs` (order, bound, batch size, gap marks, per-patch and per-region progress, lane and band); `src/jobs/ticks/tests.rs` (the hourly tick only while on); `tests/timelines.rs` (wiremock and tempfile SQLite: order and bound over three real matches and nothing asked twice; off does nothing; a 404 marked and skipped; a 429 yields until `Retry-After`; a stopped backfill resumes in order; at a 10-call app limit the backfill takes 8 and a visitor still gets 2; `match_builds` rows and a builds route that counts the game after a recompute); `src/config/tests.rs` (default 0); `src/db/tests.rs` (V0018); the OpenAPI snapshot.

## ADR-134 — A cap per job kind; analytics rebuilds one at a time (2026-10-11)
Accepted (owner issue 2026-10-11, measured on a production VPS 2026-10-10, task OPS-06). Amends design/06 §Take turns ("takes the place of a per-kind concurrency cap").
- **Why.** On a 2-vCPU, 3.7 GB VPS, riot-proxy ran 8 workers: four `aggregate:analytics` (oc1, euw1, na1, kr), `names:backfill`, `archive:match`, with 1.29 M jobs ready. The CPU was about 1 % idle, CPU pressure (PSI `some avg60`) about 48 %, memory fine. oc1's solo rebuild takes about 29 s alone (136 650 games); four started together had not finished after 7 minutes. They wait for a core and for SQLite's one write lock, and with readers always active the 205 MB WAL never checkpointed.
- **The worker count was already a setting.** The issue said `serve` had none; `JOB_CONCURRENCY` (default 8) has been one since P6. It gains a `--job-concurrency` flag, and design/07 §Sizing a note for 2-vCPU hosts (2 workers). The default stays 8 so existing deployments don't change. No `WORKERS` alias: one name per setting.
- **`JOB_KIND_LIMITS`**, `kind=n,kind=n`, default `aggregate:analytics=1`, `none` for no caps (an empty value counts as unset in this config, so it can't mean "none"). Unknown kinds, `n < 1` and malformed items fail the boot like any bad setting. The caps go to the claim as a JSON object (`$4`): a kind with as many `running` rows as its limit is left out of every lane's head, so the next row of another kind is claimed instead, and the capped kind's rows stay `pending`. The count is taken inside the claim's write, so two workers can't both take the last slot. A worker that finds only capped work sleeps as it does when every lane is blocked. The running job's own worker claims the next rebuild when it finishes.
- **Only kinds that don't call Riot may be capped** (`lanes::LANED` is refused). A capped laned kind would leave each head's `jobs_lane_claim` seek scanning past that kind's whole backlog, a million `archive:match` rows on the VPS, on every claim on the single writer. Those kinds are paced by the limiter already (SCH-01), and a crawl's share of workers is SCH-02's per-group cap. So the issue's optional `archive:match` cap is not done here; on a small host, fewer workers bound it.
- **Recompute keeps its place.** `POST /v1/admin/analytics/recompute` still queues at priority 0 and promotes a queued duplicate. With another ladder's rebuild running, its job waits as the first `aggregate:analytics` in line, and other kinds run meanwhile.
- **Queue view.** `GET /v1/admin/jobs/queue` gains `heldKinds` (kinds at their cap, from the running rows) and `held` (their ready rows, counted in `ready` too); the held rows stay in `next` as pending. The dashboard's queue panel and `/dev/jobs` add "N waiting their turn (kind)".
- **Not measured here.** The VPS acceptance (CPU pressure with `JOB_CONCURRENCY=2`; four rebuilds one after another at about 30 s each) needs the owner's host; the tests show the order and that only one runs.
- **Tests:** `src/config/tests.rs` (default, `none`, parsing, each error, the flag over the variable); `src/jobs/lanes.rs` (no kind outside `LANED` gets a lane); `src/db/store.rs` (the claim's plan keeps `jobs_lane_claim` and counts on `jobs_lane_running`); `src/jobs/scheduler/tests.rs` (four rebuilds claimed one at a time in queue order while other kinds run; no cap and a cap of 2; racing claims take the last slot once; the view's `heldKinds`/`held`; four workers never run two rebuilds); `tests/ladder.rs` (the route's `heldKinds`, `held`, `next`); `tests/dom/{dashboard,jobs}.test.mjs`; the OpenAPI snapshot.
## ADR-135 — Disk usage is measured and exported (2026-10-13)
Accepted (owner alert 2026-10-13: the VPS's disk at 92 % and rising; task DSK-01, the first of the DSK series).
- **Why.** The 20.26 GB database and a 9.9 GB daily backup had filled a 38 GB disk, and nothing in the process said so: `/v1/admin/stats` summed match bodies only (2.6 GB of the file), `/readyz` had no sizes, and the WAL, the free list and the disk's free space were invisible. The DSK tasks that follow free pages rather than bytes (design/07 §Reclaiming space), so without a free-list reading there would be nothing to show for them.
- **What.** `db::disk::usage` takes one reading: `PRAGMA page_size`, `page_count` and `freelist_count` on a reader connection, the sizes of the database file and its `-wal`, and `statvfs` of the data directory (`f_bavail`, the space an unprivileged writer can use, which is what `df` calls *Avail*). Four gauges, named as design/07 and ADR-014 have them, without v1's `proxy_` prefix: `sqlite_db_bytes`, `sqlite_wal_bytes` (planned since P7-05), `sqlite_freelist_bytes`, `data_dir_free_bytes`. They are sampled on the metrics-history tick (`METRICS_HISTORY_INTERVAL_S`, 60 s) and once when the sampler starts, so a running process never shows the boot-time 0. `GET /v1/admin/stats` adds the same reading with the page geometry: `dbFileBytes`, `walBytes`, `dbPageSize`, `dbPageCount`, `dbFreelistPages`, `diskFreeBytes`, `diskTotalBytes`.
- **Not per table.** Per-table sizes need the `dbstat` virtual table, which rusqlite's bundled SQLite does not compile in; the `sqlite3` CLI on the host has it, and design/07 §Reclaiming space gives the query. The same section documents how a file is actually shrunk (`VACUUM INTO` and swap), since no DSK task returns space to the OS by itself.
- **Tests:** `src/db/disk.rs` (a fresh database: page size 4096, fewer free pages than pages (V0019 drops a table), file = pages × page size, a positive free-space reading; 200 deleted rows show as free pages and the file keeps its size; the WAL reads 0 after a truncating checkpoint; a missing file reads 0 and a missing directory is an error); `tests/admin_routes.rs` (the fields, live); `src/metrics.rs` (the catalogue rows in `metrics.md`); the OpenAPI snapshot.
## ADR-136 — Backups are configurable and never fill the disk (2026-10-13)
Accepted (owner decision 2026-10-13, task DSK-02). Amends ADR-057; design/07 §Backups & restore, config table.
- **Why.** ADR-057 keeps 14 daily `VACUUM INTO` copies beside the database. That is right for the deployments design/07 sized (a few GB) and wrong for a 20 GB archive on a 38 GB disk: one copy was 9.9 GiB, and the next, with 1.3 GB free, would have failed part-way. Worse, the step's error retried the whole job with backoff (`JobError::Retry`), so every attempt refilled the disk with a `.partial` and the trims, the sweep and the checkpoint never ran.
- **What.** `BACKUP_KEEP` (0–365, default 14) replaces the constant; 0 takes no backup and prunes none, for a box that copies off-box by hand (`riot-proxy backup <path>`, rclone). Before the copy the step reads the free space of the backup directory's file system (DSK-01's `disk::free_bytes`) and, under 1.2 × (file + WAL), skips with a warning: the copy is about the file's live size and the day's writes need room too. A copy or rename that fails is logged, its partial removed, and the run goes on; `Report` says what happened (`backup_skipped`, `backup_failed`). Only the trims and the checkpoint still return an error, since there is nothing to go on to.
- **Not a smaller backup.** An incremental or compressed backup would be a new subsystem; Litestream (design/09) is the off-box answer when one is wanted. The owner's VPS runs with `BACKUP_KEEP=0` until the raw data leaves the box.
- **Tests:** `tests/maintenance.rs` (keep 0 writes and prunes nothing yet trims; one byte short of 1.2 × the size skips with no partial and the rest runs, with room the same run writes; a directory the service may not write to fails the copy, leaves nothing and the rest runs; the keep-14 cases as before); `src/config/tests.rs` (default and range).

## ADR-137 — Pending jobs can be purged in bulk; the queue has no in-memory mirror (2026-10-13)
Accepted (owner decision 2026-10-13, task DSK-03).
- **Why.** Four crawls finished on the VPS and left 1,126,447 pending `archive:match` rows (≈ 400 MiB with the table's four indexes) that bulk-ceiling 0 holds anyway. Cancelling a crawl drops only its own ladder legs: once handed off, an `archive:match` names just its match (ADR-087). `DELETE /v1/admin/jobs/{id}` is one row at a time, and `maintenance` trims `done` rows only. A stopped service and a hand-typed `DELETE` would have done it, but not repeatably and not with a count first.
- **What.** `Queue::purge` and `DELETE /v1/admin/jobs?kind=&state=&minPriority=&dryRun=`: `kind` from `kinds::ALL`, `state` `pending` or `failed` (a `running` row belongs to its worker; `done` rows are `maintenance`'s), `minPriority` the smallest priority number to delete (a lower band is a larger number), `dryRun` counts only. The answer is `{kind, state, minPriority, matched, deleted, dryRun}` and a deleting call logs a warning. Rows go in chunks of 5 000 per write with 20 ms between, so a million-row backlog takes seconds of small writes rather than one write that holds the lock for them all. A row claimed between two chunks is `running` and stays.
- **The band protects a visitor's work.** Crawl-found matches are queued at `order::MATCH` = 20 005; a lookup's history walk at `100 + depth / 10`; a match the site asks for and TL-01's other half at 0 and 100. So `minPriority=20000` on the VPS drops the crawl backlog and nothing a person is waiting for.
- **No mirror.** design/04's diagram showed a "job priority heap (mirror of jobs table)" in process memory. There is none: a claim is one `UPDATE … RETURNING` on the table, `recover` at boot only puts `running` rows back to `pending`, and `assign_lanes` fills `lane IS NULL`. Deleting rows under a running scheduler is therefore safe, and the diagram (design/04, design/03) now says so. The deleted pages stay on SQLite's free list (design/07 §Reclaiming space), so the file does not shrink; `sqlite_freelist_bytes` (DSK-01) rises by what the rows and index entries took.
- **Tests:** `src/jobs/scheduler/tests.rs` (two chunks and a remainder deleted; a row of another kind, a lower-band row and a running row stay; a dry run deletes nothing); `tests/admin_jobs.rs` (the route with `matched`/`deleted` before and after, the band, a running row surviving, failed rows, the admin scope, each validation error); the OpenAPI snapshot.

## ADR-138 — A participant's tier lives on their fact row (2026-10-13)
Accepted (owner decision 2026-10-13, task DSK-04). Amends ADR-127 (THR-02) and ADR-129's "joins `match_tiers`"; design/04 §Schema.
- **Why.** `match_tiers` was 825 MiB of the VPS's 20 GB: 330 MiB of rows that repeated the fact's key (a 78-byte puuid and the match id), a platform the match id already names, a queue `matches.queue_id` already holds, and a stamp time nothing but tests read; plus three indexes (the key's 253 MiB, `match_tiers_ladder` 110 MiB with no reader at all, `match_tiers_unknown` 132 MiB for the late stamp). The value it carried is one short string per participant.
- **What.** V0019 adds `match_facts.tier`, copies each stamp by key and drops the table. NULL means not stamped; `tiers:backfill` now looks for a ranked match with any fact at NULL (before: a match with no stamp rows). A stamp is an `UPDATE … WHERE tier IS NULL`, so a re-archive keeps the first one as before. The late stamp updates one player's `UNKNOWN` facts on a platform's id range (the match id's prefix) and checks the queue id and end time on `matches` by key; it reads `facts_player` and nothing new. Analytics read `f.tier` where they joined `mt.tier`, and skip facts at NULL, as they skipped facts without a stamp row. Output is byte-identical (the analytics fixtures prove it).
- **ADR-127's "facts are a pure derivation" is kept by the upsert, not by a second table.** `facts::write` no longer deletes a match's rows before re-inserting them: it upserts every column except `tier`, then deletes only this scope's rows for participants the body no longer names (there are none in practice; Riot's participant list is fixed). So `facts:reextract` rewrites every derived column and leaves the stamp where it was, which is what the separate table was for. `tiers.rs` is still the only writer of the column.
- **Not denormalised.** No `platform`, `queue` or `stamped_at` on the fact: the first two every reader binds already, the third had no reader. No index on `tier`: analytics group the ladder's facts by it after reading them by match, which is what they did with the join.
- **Migration cost.** One `UPDATE` over 2.3 M rows with a key lookup each, then the drop: minutes on the VPS's two cores and about 0.7 GiB of WAL until the next checkpoint; the dropped pages go to the free list (design/07 §Reclaiming space).
- **Tests:** `src/archive/tiers/tests.rs` (every THR-02 case on the column: stamps once per participant, never restamped, ADR-105's newer-wins, other queues and platforms ignored, non-ranked matches not stamped, re-extraction leaves the stamps, late placement inside the window only, a ladder page's placement; new: one unstamped fact brings its match back to the backfill; a participant gone from the body loses only their row; the late stamp's plan reads `facts_player` and each match by key, no scan); `src/archive/analytics/tests.rs` (the fixtures byte-identical; the matchups and facts plans join no tier table); `src/db/tests.rs` (V0019 seeded at version 18: stamps copied, an unstamped fact NULL and found, a non-ranked match's fact NULL and not found, the table gone; the migration counts).

## ADR-139 — Rune pages are stored as a positional array (2026-10-13)
Accepted (owner decision 2026-10-13, task DSK-05). design/04 §Schema.
- **Why.** `match_facts.runes` held a keyed object, `{"primaryStyle":8000,"keystone":8010,"subStyle":8400,"perks":[…6],"statPerks":[…3]}`, 122 bytes of which 60 are the five key names, on 2.3 M rows: 345 MiB of JSON across the three columns, most of it runes. `items` (`[3181,…]`, 31 bytes) and `summoners` (`[4,14]`) are already bare arrays.
- **What.** Version 4 of the facts writes `[primaryStyle, keystone, subStyle, [perks…], [offense, flex, defense] or null]`, about 65 bytes, the same values re-encoded: nothing new is read from Riot and no field is reinterpreted. The analytics SQL reads `'$[1]'` and `'$[2]'` where it read `'$.keystone'` and `'$.subStyle'`. No route returns the column; the match page's rune block comes from the body. `FACTS_VERSION` 4, so the boot catch-up queues `facts:reextract` (priority 30 000, `FACTS_REEXTRACT_BATCH`, 50 ms between batches, `proxy_facts_reextract_progress`) and every old row is rewritten from its body, keeping its tier (ADR-138). About 230 k bodies on the VPS: ten to twenty minutes.
- **Why not more.** A packed BLOB would be smaller still but breaks `json_extract`/`json_each` in the rebuild SQL; separate INTEGER columns cost the same bytes as the array with more columns to carry. A row rewritten in place frees no disk (a leaf page is merged only when it drops below a third full), so this pays only when the table is copied: DSK-07's rebuild of `match_facts`, which must deploy after this sweep has finished.
- **Tests:** `src/archive/facts/tests.rs` (the stored array in order, the keystone at `$[1]`; the three insta snapshots re-accepted in the new shape); `src/archive/analytics/tests.rs` (the fixtures seeded as arrays give the same stats, runes and build parts); `tests/analytics.rs` (a sweep over version-2 rows with keyed-object runes and a `MASTER` stamp leaves every row at version 4, with array runes and the stamp); `tests/migrate_v1.rs` (a v1 import lands at version 4).

## ADR-140 — Derived tables keyed by their whole row are WITHOUT ROWID (2026-10-13)
Accepted (owner decision 2026-10-13, task DSK-06). design/04 §Schema.
- **Why.** SQLite's default table is a b-tree on a hidden rowid; a declared primary key is a second b-tree (`sqlite_autoindex_*`) holding the key columns again. For `match_builds` (key: match id, key scope, puuid), `match_bans` (match id, team, turn) and the analytics tables (key scope, platform, queue, patch, champion, role, …, tier, side) the key is most or all of the row, so the index was as big as the table: 460 MiB of the VPS's 20 GB (match_builds 164, match_bans 63, the analytics autoindexes 234).
- **What.** V0020 rebuilds the thirteen tables `WITHOUT ROWID`, copying rows in key order (V0017's pattern) so the new b-tree is packed, then drops the old table and renames. The DDL is otherwise unchanged; `champion_stats_slice` is recreated. No code changes: `builds::write`, `facts::write_bans` and `Scope::clear`/`insert` delete and insert by key already, and nothing reads these tables by rowid.
- **Which tables, and which not.** A `WITHOUT ROWID` table's secondary indexes carry the primary key instead of an 8-byte rowid, so the rule is: tables with no secondary index, or whose indexes already contain the key. Of these thirteen only `champion_stats` has one (`champion_stats_slice`, 4 MiB), which now carries the key's four remaining columns, a few MiB. `match_facts` is not here: `facts_player` would not grow (it holds the key's columns), but `facts_champ` would, and the rows are over SQLite's rule-of-thumb size until DSK-05 packs them; DSK-07 takes it on. `matches`, `timelines` and `jobs` keep their rowids: large blobs and a lane index.
- **Cost.** A copy of 0.5 GiB of rows on the VPS: a minute or three of boot, as much WAL until the next checkpoint, and the old pages on the free list (design/07 §Reclaiming space).
- **Tests:** `src/db/tests.rs` (V0020 seeded at version 19: every table's rows survive with their values, each DDL ends in `WITHOUT ROWID`, the slice index and both foreign keys are back and still cascade from `matches`; the migration counts); the analytics, builds and archive suites run unchanged on the new shape.

## ADR-141 — `match_facts` is WITHOUT ROWID and loses `facts_champ` (2026-10-13)
Accepted (owner decision 2026-10-13, task DSK-07). design/04 §Schema; follows ADR-140.
- **Why.** `match_facts` is keyed by (match id, puuid): 95 bytes a rowid table stored twice, once in the row and once in the key's automatic index, 253 MiB on the VPS's 2.3 M rows. `facts_champ` (champion id alone, 25 MiB) has no reader: the player page filters by key scope and puuid first (`facts_player`), analytics filter the champion inside their match joins, and the rank planner reads by key.
- **What.** V0021 copies the table `WITHOUT ROWID` in key order, with the version-4 rows DSK-05 packed, so the copy is dense; recreates `facts_player`; and leaves `facts_champ` behind. In a `WITHOUT ROWID` table a secondary index carries the primary key instead of an 8-byte rowid: `facts_player` (key scope, puuid, match id) already holds the key's columns, so it does not grow, while `facts_champ` would have grown from 25 to about 250 MiB, which is the other reason it goes. The rank planner's plan now says `USING PRIMARY KEY` where it said the autoindex's name; nothing else reads the table by rowid.
- **Rows over SQLite's rule of thumb.** The documentation suggests `WITHOUT ROWID` for rows under about a twentieth of a page (200 bytes); a fact is about 255 after DSK-05. Every access is by the key or through `facts_player`, interior pages carry 95-byte keys (a fan-out near 40, some 13 MiB of them), and the b-tree is written once in key order; accepted.
- **Order.** Deployed after DSK-05's `facts:reextract` has finished (`proxy_facts_reextract_progress` = 1): a copy taken before it would pack the old 122-byte rune objects and DSK-05's bytes would stay on the free list of the new table instead.
- **Cost.** A copy of 0.7 GiB of rows and one index build: 3–8 minutes of boot on the VPS's two cores, about 0.9 GiB of temporary space and as much WAL; the old pages go to the free list (design/07 §Reclaiming space).
- **Tests:** `src/db/tests.rs` (V0021 seeded at version 20: every row with its NULLs, JSON and tier survives in key order, the DDL says `WITHOUT ROWID`, `facts_player` is back, `facts_champ` gone, the foreign key cascades; the index list and the migration counts); `src/archive/ranks.rs` (the planner's plan reads the facts by primary key); `src/archive/matches/tests.rs` (facts read in key order); the archive, tiers and analytics suites unchanged on the new shape.

## ADR-142 — Production moves from OVH to Onidel Sydney (2026-10-13)
Accepted (owner decision 2026-10-13, task HOST-01). design/12 §Migration runbook, §As executed.
- **Why.** The OVH VPS had 2 cores, 3.7 GB of RAM and a 38 GB disk that reached 97 % on 2026-10-13 (DSK). OVH's Sydney region had no stock of a larger plan. A comparison of sixteen countries' providers (2026-10-13) found nothing cheaper than Sydney for this workload once Hetzner's April and June 2026 repricing is counted, and every overseas option adds 230–290 ms between the owner and the server and between the server and Riot's OC1 routes. The personal key's 100 requests per 2 minutes makes cores and bandwidth matter little; RAM for SQLite's page cache and disk room for the archive matter most.
- **What.** Onidel ONI-4 in Sydney (6 vCPU, 12 GB RAM, 120 GB NVMe on replicated storage, 6 TB, A$42.52 a month), named `ngf-prod-syd1`, Ubuntu 26.04 LTS. The proxy, ninjagoldfinch.lol (`node build`) and the site's Cloudflare Tunnel moved together with the 22 GB database, in a few minutes of downtime; nothing listens publicly except SSH. BinaryLane (local NVMe, A$39.20 for 4 vCPU / 8 GB) was the runner-up.
- **Backups.** The hand-made off-box job from the old box, not previously in this repository, moved with it: every 6 hours `rclone copy` of `$DATA_DIR/backups` to R2 bucket `ngf-backups` (prefix `riot-proxy/`, 14-day lifecycle expiry), `rclone check --one-way`, then a prune to one local backup. `no_head = true` avoids R2's 501 on rclone 1.60's `HEAD ?versionId=`. Set `BACKUP_KEEP=1` (ADR-136) when a release containing it is deployed.
- **The old box.** Wiped the same day once a backup was verified in R2, renamed `ngf-mon-syd1`, kept as the monitoring host (`ninja-monitor`, later Prometheus and Grafana). It holds no Riot key and no data.
- **Consequences.** Fsync costs about 3 ms (replicated storage) and cold random reads about 10 MB/s; the page cache hides both once warm. Onidel blocks outbound SSH by default, a per-server toggle the owner turned off. The database roughly doubled in a day (backup 10.6 GB on 2026-10-12, 21.6 GB on 2026-10-13), which makes moving raw bodies to object storage (design/12, OBJ proposal) the next disk lever.
- **Tests:** none; an operations change with no code. Verified on the box: `PRAGMA quick_check` = `ok`, `/readyz` ok with all SQLite readers free, the tunnel's one connector on `ngf-prod-syd1`, the backup present in R2 and passing `rclone check`.

## ADR-143 — `ME1_` match ids are served by europe (2026-10-13)
Accepted (owner report 2026-10-13, task ME-01). design/09 §Riot.
- **Why.** Production's EUW crawl queued 27 `ME1_…` match ids, which failed with "cannot derive region from match id": `ME1` is not one of the sixteen platforms in `routing.rs`, and Riot's routing table (developer.riotgames.com/docs/lol#routing-values, read 2026-10-13) does not list it either.
- **Source.** On 2026-10-13 the owner fetched `ME1_17228556` from each regional host with the production key: `europe` 200, `americas`, `asia` and `sea` 404.
- **What.** `MATCH_ONLY_PREFIXES` maps `ME1` to europe, and `Region::from_match_id` falls back to it when the prefix is no platform. `archive:match`, its rate-limit lane and the `/lol/match` routes' region check use it, so an `ME1_` match archives with `matches.region = 'europe'`, counts against europe's match-v5 limits, and a request naming another region is refused with the usual `BAD_REGION`.
- **Not a platform.** No `me1` platform host is documented, so ME1 stays out of `Platform`: platform routes, ladders, rank lookups and tier stamps (no ME1 ladder is crawled, so its participants stay unstamped) do not accept or use it. A later owner request with a source can add it.
- **Tests:** `src/riot/routing.rs` (`ME1_` in any case → europe and no platform; unknown and malformed prefixes none); `src/jobs/lanes.rs` (the archive lane is europe's); `src/routes/lol.rs` (europe accepted, another region refused naming europe); `tests/archive_jobs.rs` (an `ME1_` match archives with region `europe`).

## ADR-144 — The admin dashboard is API-first, embedded, and reached through Cloudflare Access (2026-10-13)
Accepted (owner decision 2026-10-13, task ADM-00). design/13 §Reach; design/12 §Admin hostname behind Access.
- **Why.** `/dashboard` is v1's page ported verbatim (ADR-038). It shows no disk, backups, traffic, errors, consumers or build (the disk reached 97 % on 2026-10-13 unseen), polls four endpoints while hidden, and every snapshot, per request and per 5 s `metrics` tick, counts `matches` and groups the whole `jobs` table (1.1 M rows on production). The owner asked for a page that is better looking, far more informative, faster and easier to use. The series is ADM (IMPLEMENTATION.md §Post-release — ADM).
- **What.** The page stays one hand-written file embedded in the binary (`src/ui/admin.html` from ADM-08), served at `/dashboard` behind `DASHBOARD_UI`, with no framework, build step or other origin (ADR-071, ADR-080), at most 160 KB. It is API-first: everything it shows comes from `/v1/admin/*` JSON and the `metrics` and `firehose` WebSocket topics; the server renders only the page bar. It reads its base URL from one setting (default `location.origin`) and the admin key from `localStorage`. Expensive aggregates move to a background sampler (ADM-02); the JSON shapes only gain keys; Prometheus names and the Grafana dashboard are unchanged. Grafana stays the history view; this page is the "now and act" view.
- **Reach.** Production serves the page and the admin API on `admin.ninjagoldfinch.lol`, a second tunnel route to the same `127.0.0.1:8080`, behind a Cloudflare Access self-hosted application with One-time PIN login allowing only the owner. Page and API share one origin, so there is no CORS and the admin key never crosses origins; the Access cookie and the admin key are two gates. The proxy gets no login system of its own; ADM-18 may later trust the Access JWT instead of the key.
- **Not chosen.** A separate service or static host for the page: a second deploy pipeline, version skew between page and API, CORS (deferred in ADR-011 and ADR-014) and an admin key sent cross-origin, for a file of about 100 KB in a 16.6 MB binary. The page keeps the option: ADM-07 adds an opt-in `ADMIN_CORS_ORIGINS`, and the page needs no change to run from another origin. In that split the admin API on `api.` would be key-only, since Access cookies are per hostname.
- **Finding.** The tunnel's `api.ninjagoldfinch.lol` route has path `*`, so `/metrics` (no auth), `/dashboard` and `/v1/admin/*` are public there today, and `ADMIN_IP_ALLOWLIST` is blind behind the tunnel (`TRUST_PROXY=false` sees cloudflared's `127.0.0.1`). ADM-01 blocks `/metrics`, `/dashboard*`, `/dev*` and `/v1/admin*` on `api.` with a WAF custom rule; `/v1/ws` stays, its admin topics already need the admin key.
- **Consequences.** Between ADM-08 and ADM-12 the new page has no Ladder tab; `/dev/jobs` and the ladder admin routes cover it. `/dev/jobs` and `/dashboard/config.json` are retired in ADM-17.
- **Tests:** none; documentation only. Each ADM task ships its own.

## ADR-145 — The dashboard's aggregates are sampled in the background (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-02). design/13 §Sampling; design/04 §Schema.
- **Why.** Every `GET /v1/admin/metrics` and every 5 s `metrics` tick counted `matches`, grouped the whole `jobs` table (1.13 M pending rows on production) and read the ladder and analytics tables; `GET /v1/admin/stats` also summed the size of every archived match body. On a copy of production's job table the queue count alone took about 950 ms.
- **What.** `Stats` keeps two samples behind `RwLock`s, read by one `spawn_sampler`: a fast set (totals, queues, ladder, analytics) at boot and every 15 s, and a slow set (the timelines count and the archive's byte sums, disk usage and the DSK-01 gauges) at boot and with each history point, which samples both and is built from them. Analytics is in the fast set, not the slow one design/13 first proposed: its read is one ladder's rows, and the acceptance suite waits at most 60 s for a finished recompute to show in the snapshot. The snapshot reads the samples; only in-memory parts (limiter, WebSocket and event counts, cache counters, flows, process) are computed per request. Before the first sample, as in test apps, the parts are read live.
- **`/v1/admin/stats`.** Its match and player counts stay live: they are index counts, and callers poll them for progress (the acceptance suite waits on `archivedMatches` as a backfill drains). The timelines count, the archive's byte sums (a pass over every stored body) and the disk readings come from the slow sample.
- **`sampledAt`.** The snapshot and `/v1/admin/stats` gain `sampledAt {fast, slow}`, unix ms, always present. A live read carries the request's time. Additive: no existing key changes; the two tests that list the snapshot's keys gain it.
- **Index.** V0022 adds `jobs_state_kind ON jobs(state, kind, run_after, finished_at)`. The queue count is rewritten to group by state then kind, in the index's order, and it, `GET /v1/admin/jobs/stats` and `jobs_pending{kind}` read only the index (a test checks the three query plans). Measured on 1.19 M rows: the index is about 44 MB (38 bytes a row), the queue count falls to about 240 ms, `jobs_pending` to about 100 ms. Each job state change now writes one more index entry; the claim and lane indexes already did the same, and the trade is cheap next to a full table read every 5 s per watcher. The cost shrinks once the stale backlog is purged (DSK-03).
- **Freshness.** Queue counts and analytics can be 15 s old, archive bytes and disk one history interval old. The page shows the age from `sampledAt`; Grafana and `/metrics` are unaffected.
- **Tests:** `tests/dashboard.rs` (rows written after a sample appear in the snapshot only after the next one, in `/v1/admin/stats`'s counts at once; its bytes and disk are the slow sample's; `sampledAt` is the samples' time; the history point equals the samples); `src/db/tests.rs` (V0022 applied, the index exists, the three job counts use it as a covering index); the OpenAPI snapshot.

## ADR-146 — Raw bodies go to object storage in packs (2026-10-13)
Accepted (owner decisions 2026-10-13, task OBJ-00). design/12 §Storage layout, §Owner answers.
- **Why.** design/12's first draft stored each match and timeline body as its own object. Onidel's knowledge base caps a bucket at 3 million objects by default; at two objects per match the archive (230 k matches today, 10–20 k more a day) passes that within months. The owner preferred batching in general: fewer objects make the R2 replica (billed per write), any later move between providers and every listing cheap.
- **What.** Bodies are packed back to back into objects of about 64 MiB (`ARCHIVE_PACK_TARGET_BYTES`), matches and timelines in separate packs (they are read separately: reextract and the player page want matches, `builds:extract` timelines) and sorted by match id inside each (the bulk jobs walk in match-id order, so a batch falls in few packs), each ending in a zstd-compressed index and a 16-byte trailer, so a pack describes itself. SQLite's `body_locations` (match id, kind → pack, offset, length, sha256) says where each offloaded body is; `body_packs` tracks each pack's size and live bytes. A read is one ranged GET of the body's bytes, checked against its sha256.
- **Bytes are unchanged.** Ingress is free on both stores and a ranged read fetches only its body, so egress is what one object per body would have cost. The gain is in object and request counts: about 250 packs for today's 16 GB (about 40 of matches, 210 of timelines) instead of 460 k objects.
- **Upload and free are separate.** A body is packed and uploaded as soon as a full pack of its kind is ready (hours), keeping its local copy; the copy is freed when the body leaves the 14-day window (League's patch cycle). So new matches reach Onidel within hours and R2 within the hour after, and freeing is a database-only step.
- **Recovery.** The location map is also snapshotted to the bucket daily (`index/locations-<date>.jsonl.zst` and `index/packs-<date>.jsonl.zst`, 14-day expiry), so a lost database and backup recover from one snapshot plus the packs written since, not every pack. The R2 replica runs hourly, which bounds how long a new pack exists on one provider.
- **Deletes.** A deletion drops the location and lowers the pack's live bytes; compaction (OBJ-06) rewrites packs under half live and deletes the old object. Bodies are immutable and deletions rare.
- **Also decided.** Onidel Sydney 250 GB as primary (annual billing, A$21.60 a year, upgradable, not downgradable), R2 as replica, a 14-day local window, the `object_store` crate.
- **Tests:** none; a design change. OBJ-01..06 each ship their own.

## ADR-147 — Build identity and `GET /v1/admin/info` (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-03). design/07 §The artefact; design/13 §What the page talks to.
- **Why.** Nothing told an operator which commit production runs, when it started or what configuration is in effect; the new dashboard's health strip and About tab need all three, and `--version` only names the release.
- **What.** `build.rs` sets `GIT_SHA` (the environment's, else `git rev-parse HEAD`, else `unknown`, cut to 12 characters), `RUSTC_VERSION` and `BUILD_UNIX_MS` (`SOURCE_DATE_EPOCH` when set). It reruns when `GIT_SHA` changes or, in a checkout, when `HEAD` or its branch ref moves, and it cannot fail a build. `GET /v1/admin/info` (admin scope) returns `version`, `gitSha`, `rustc`, `builtAt`, `startedAt`, `uptimeSeconds`, `env`, `role`, `keyScope`, `features`, `workers` and `config`.
- **`config`.** `Config::redacted()` maps every name in `config::VARS` but `RIOT_API_KEY` to the value in effect, spelled as `.env` would: `BOOTSTRAP_ADMIN_KEY` and a Postgres `DATABASE_URL` read `[REDACTED]`, unset optional values are null. The Riot key is left out entirely, not masked, so not even its presence or length shows. A test pins the key set to `VARS`, so a new variable has to be added here.
- **Builds.** The CI, edge and release workflows set `GIT_SHA: ${{ github.sha }}` and grep the built binary for it, so a build that silently reads `unknown` fails. The source `Dockerfile` takes `ARG GIT_SHA` (`.git` is not in its context) and compose passes `${GIT_SHA:-}`. `--version` is unchanged; release.yml's check on it still holds.
- **Tests:** `tests/admin_routes.rs` (admin 200 with the build and config, reader 403, the body holds no `rpx_`, `RGAPI`, bootstrap key or Riot key); `src/config/tests.rs` (the key set, masking); `src/build_info.rs` (the script set every value); the OpenAPI snapshot.

## ADR-148 — Runtime gauges in the dashboard's snapshot (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-04). design/13 §Runtime gauges; design/metrics.md.
- **Why.** The new dashboard's Limiter, Cache, Consumers and Archive tabs and its health strip need readings the snapshot did not carry: how full the cache tiers are, what the bulk gate holds, who is using their quota, the read pool and the disk.
- **What.** The snapshot gains `cacheStore`, `gates`, `inflight`, `quotas`, `sqlite` and `disk`, and each `limiter[]` scope gains `interactiveWaiters`. All are read from memory per snapshot except `cacheStore.l2Rows` and `disk`, which come from ADM-02's slow sample. `Stats` holds the fetcher (cache and single-flight), the quotas and the config, so `serve` and the test app build the quotas and an `Arc<Config>` before it.
- **Quotas.** `Quotas::usage()` reads every consumer's window without taking a slot, busiest first, keyed by consumer id; the page joins names from `/v1/admin/consumers`. Admin requests are metered like any other, so the admin's own polling shows.
- **Dropped L2 writes.** The write-behind queue already dropped an entry when full and logged it; it now counts them, in the writer (`l2Dropped`) and as the counter `cache_l2_dropped_total` (a v2 name, ADR-014 style). `L2Writer::spawn_queued` takes the queue size for the test.
- **History.** Points gain `disk`, `requests` and `upstream` as `#[serde(default)]` options, so the 1440 points already stored keep parsing, with the additions null. The request totals are counters since the start: the page draws rates from differences and treats a drop as a restart.
- **Additive.** No existing key changes; the two tests that list the snapshot's keys and the one that lists a history point's gain the new ones. Prometheus names are unchanged but for the new counter; the Grafana dashboard is untouched.
- **Tests:** `tests/dashboard.rs` (an L1 entry, a frozen scope and a consumer's requests show in their sections; gates carry the config; a pre-ADM-04 point is served with null additions; a new point carries the disk and totals); `tests/cache_l2.rs` (a full queue drops and counts, and the queued writes still land); the metric catalogue test; the OpenAPI snapshot.

## ADR-149 — Recent requests and errors, kept in memory (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-05). design/13 §Recent requests and errors.
- **Why.** The operator could see request counts in Prometheus but not the requests themselves: which call just failed, with what message and request id, for which consumer, how slow. The logs hold it, but not where the dashboard can show it.
- **What.** Two process-global bounded rings (`http::recent`, a `OnceLock` like the metrics recorder): the last 500 requests, pushed by `app::record_request` with the route template, the path cut to 200 characters, status, `X-Cache` state, latency, the consumer's name and the request id; and the last 200 error envelopes, pushed by `ApiError::into_response` with code, status, message and request id. `GET /v1/admin/recent?kind=requests|errors&limit=1..500` (admin) reads them newest first.
- **Privacy.** Nothing that is not already in the request log line is kept: no body, header, query string or key. The consumer is named by the auth layer through a response extension, so a public route or a refused key reads null.
- **Skipped.** `/healthz`, `/readyz`, `/metrics`, `/v1/admin/metrics` and `/v1/admin/recent` are not recorded, so the ring shows traffic rather than the probes and the page watching it. Their errors still are.
- **Snapshot.** `requests` and `upstream` give `proxy_requests_total` and `proxy_upstream_requests_total` by label, joined with `|` because route templates hold `:`. Additive; the two key-list tests gain them.
- **Not persisted.** A restart empties the rings; history across restarts is Grafana's and the logs'. A database table would add a write per request for a view that is about the last minutes.
- **Tests:** `tests/admin_routes.rs` (three requests read back by their `X-Request-Id`, newest first, with route, status, cache and consumer; probes and polling absent; a 404 and a 403 in the error ring and a 500 raised in a request scope; bad `kind` and `limit` refused; `/info` and `/recent` in the admin-scope list). The rings are shared across a test binary, so tests find their own entries by id and never assert emptiness. `src/http/recent.rs` (caps, skip list, path cut); `tests/dashboard.rs` (`requests` counts a served route).

## ADR-150 — Backup status and maintenance on demand (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-06). design/07 §Backups & restore; design/13 §What the page talks to.
- **Why.** Whether last night's backup was written, skipped for lack of disk (ADR-136) or failed was only in the logs; the dashboard's health strip and Archive & Storage tab need it, and the operator wanted a way to run maintenance after freeing space without waiting a day.
- **What.** `Maintenance` remembers its last run (`LastRun {at, ms, report, error}`) behind a lock, read by `last()`; `run_once` records it whether the run succeeded or failed, and a skipped or failed backup shows in the report as before. `AppState` holds the `Arc<Maintenance>` the scheduler uses. `GET /v1/admin/backups` returns `{dir, keep, files[{name, bytes, modifiedAt}], lastRun, diskFreeBytes}`: the files come from `maintenance::backups` and their metadata, never from opening one, and a missing directory is an empty list. `POST /v1/admin/maintenance/run` enqueues `maintenance` with the tick's dedupe key and answers 202 `{ok, jobId, status}`, `already-queued` when one is waiting.
- **Report.** `Report` now serialises in camelCase and is in the OpenAPI document; it was only ever logged with `Debug`, so nothing depended on its old field names.
- **Not persisted.** The last run is this process's: a restart forgets it until the next run. The files on disk, and R2 for the off-box copies (ADR-142), remain the record.
- **Tests:** `tests/maintenance.rs` (`last()` is `None` before a run, holds the report for a normal, a skipped and a failed-copy run, and the error with no report for a failed run); `tests/admin_routes.rs` (no directory is `files: []`; two seeded backups newest first, a stray file ignored; the last run in camelCase; run-now queues then joins; both routes need the admin scope); the OpenAPI snapshot.

## ADR-151 — Opt-in CORS for the admin API (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-07). design/13 §Reach; design/07 §Configuration. Closes the deferral of ADR-011 and ADR-014.
- **Why.** The dashboard is same-origin on production (ADR-144), but the page is written to run from another origin (its base URL setting), and a browser on another origin cannot read the admin API without CORS.
- **What.** `ADMIN_CORS_ORIGINS`, a CSV of exact origins (`http(s)://host[:port]`, no path); empty, the default, sends no `Access-Control-*` header on any response. `*` is refused at boot: the admin API takes a key, and any page could then use one pasted into it. When set, `http::cors::scoped` applies a `CorsLayer` (the listed origins; GET, POST, DELETE; `Authorization` and `Content-Type`; exposes `X-Request-Id`; max-age 600 s) to `/v1/admin/*`, `/v1/ws`, `/healthz` and `/readyz`, the paths the page reads. Every other path passes through untouched, so `/v1/riot/*`, `/v1/lol/*` and the read routes never send CORS headers.
- **Placement.** The middleware wraps the whole router, outside the routes' auth `route_layer` and outside the 404 and method-not-allowed fallbacks: axum answers an `OPTIONS` no route handles from those fallbacks, so a layer on the admin router alone saw no preflight (it 404ed). A preflight therefore needs no key; the request that follows still does, and its 401 or 403 carries the CORS headers so the page can read it.
- **Dependency.** `tower` moves from dev-dependencies to dependencies (same version and `util` feature axum already pulls in), to call the layered service; `Cargo.lock` and the binary are unchanged.
- **Health routes.** Added beside the spec's admin group and `/v1/ws`, because the page's health strip reads `/readyz`.
- **Tests:** `tests/cors.rs` (unset: no CORS header on a preflight, an authorised and an unauthorised admin call, `/readyz` or `/v1/ws`; set: preflights on admin, `/v1/ws` and `/readyz` answered without a key with the origin, methods, headers and max-age; a 401 carries the origin; `X-Request-Id` exposed; a second listed origin; an unlisted origin gets no `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`; read routes get no CORS header, preflight or not); `src/config/tests.rs` (default off, a list parsed, `*`, paths, a trailing slash, another scheme and a bare host refused); `src/http/cors.rs` (the paths it covers).

## ADR-152 — The new dashboard shell replaces v1's page (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-08). design/13 §Shell, §Layout, §Visual tokens. Supersedes ADR-038's verbatim port of v1's dashboard.
- **What.** `src/ui/admin.html` is served at `/dashboard`; v1's `dashboard.html` and its three test files are deleted (v1 parity is retired). Layout A: a left rail of tabs that becomes a scrolling tab strip under 820 px, a sticky health strip, and only the active tab rendered. The page is one file with inline CSS custom-property tokens and one inline module script, no framework, no build step, nothing from another origin, at most 160 KB.
- **API-first, as ADR-144 set out.** The probe is `GET /v1/admin/info`, not `/dashboard/config.json` (left in place until ADM-17); 200 starts the page, so `AUTH_DISABLED` needs no special case. Every call goes through one base URL setting. The socket subscribes to `metrics` and `firehose` as v1's page did, and drops `metrics` while the tab is hidden, which stops the server building snapshots for it. No polling timers but a 1 s clock.
- **Tokens.** The spec's tokens, with two changes measured in `tests/admin_ui.mjs`: the light theme's muted ink is `#6b6a66` (`#898781` is 3.4:1 on the light page, under WCAG AA), and the light theme's status colours are darker steps (`#0b8a0b #a86f00 #c2521b #d03b3b`), since the dark theme's fall to 1.8–3.4:1 on white. Status is always an icon and words. The dark theme is unchanged.
- **Caching.** `/dashboard` is sent with a strong `ETag` and `Cache-Control: no-cache` instead of `no-store`: a reload revalidates and an unchanged page is a 304.
- **Gap.** The rail lists only built tabs: the Overview now, the rest one task at a time (ADM-09..15). Until ADM-12 there is no Ladder tab and the old page's crawl-phase check (`tests/dashboard.rs`) is gone with it; `/dev/jobs`, `/v1/admin/ladder/*` and the firehose cover crawls meanwhile, and ADM-12 brings the check back.
- **Tests:** `tests/dom/admin-shell.test.mjs` (the probe and key sheet, refused and read-only keys, `AUTH_DISABLED`, the router and tablist roles, the strip and tiles from the first snapshot, a `metrics.snapshot` frame fanning out, hidden and visible, socket backoff and a 4401 close, one interval only, theme, base URL, sign out, `confirmAction`, toasts, the table helper); `tests/dom/admin.browser.test.mjs` (Chromium at 1280, 820, 390 px in both themes); `tests/admin_ui.mjs` (helpers and token contrast); `tests/ui.rs` (self-contained, budget, one module script, the `id="app"` marker, `ETag` and 304, `APEX_LIST_CAP`).

## ADR-153 — Dashboard charts and the Traffic tab (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-09). design/13 §Overview charts and Traffic, §Chart rules.
- **Charts.** Hand-drawn SVG in the page, no library: one `<path>` per series however many points the history holds (drawn from at most 600 bucket means, so the 1440-point history is one short path), a wash for a single series, a crosshair readout by pointer and keyboard, an `aria-label` description and a Table toggle, per design/13 §Chart rules. The light theme's palette warns that three series fall under 3:1 on white, which the validator says obliges visible values or a table view: each chart prints its latest value and has the table.
- **Rates from history.** Points carry cumulative counters (cache reads, and since ADM-04 request totals), so per-interval rates and hit ratios come from neighbouring points; a counter that falls marks a restart and leaves a gap rather than a negative rate.
- **Live end.** The history route is read once per entry; snapshots then extend it, one point per history interval with the newest snapshot as the live end, also after re-entry.
- **Traffic polling.** The recent ring is polled every 5 s only while the Traffic tab is open and visible, as a `setTimeout` chain cleared on leave and on hide; the Overview still has no timer but the 1 s clock. Its errors panel refreshes when a snapshot counts new 4xx or 5xx answers, not on a timer.
- **Latency.** p50 and p95 per route are computed in the page from the last 500 requests (nearest rank), not by the server: the ring already holds them, and a histogram per route would add Prometheus series.
- **Tests:** `tests/dom/admin-overview.test.mjs` (six charts of one path each from 1440 points, history fetched once per entry, the pre-ADM-04 disk gap, a snapshot extending the charts, keyboard readout, table toggle capped at 200, errors refreshed on new 4xx/5xx, too little history, re-entry keeping the live end); `tests/dom/admin-traffic.test.mjs` (by-route counts, hit share and percentiles, Riot calls, filters, status icons, polling only while open and visible); `tests/dom/admin.browser.test.mjs` (both views at three widths in both themes, the crosshair under the pointer); `tests/admin_ui.mjs` (the new helpers).

## ADR-154 — Limiter and Cache tabs; a dry run for the cache purge (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-10). design/13 §Limiter and Cache.
- **Purge dry run.** `POST /v1/admin/cache/purge` takes an optional `dryRun`: it counts the memory keys and persisted rows the pattern matches (`L1::keys_where`, `l2::keys_where`, read-only) and answers `{ok, pattern, matched, dryRun: true}`, deleting nothing. Without it the route is unchanged. The page shows the count before a purge can be confirmed, so nobody deletes an unknown number of entries.
- **Meters.** Every limit is drawn as a meter whose numbers are text and whose fill colour follows severity (accent, warning from 80 %, critical from 95 %) with an icon from 80 %, so no state is colour alone. The thresholds mirror the bulk ceiling's default (0.8).
- **Order.** Scopes that need a look come first: frozen, then holding bulk work, then by fullness. Method buckets are sorted by their fullest window, four shown and the rest folded.
- **No polling.** The Limiter and Cache tabs redraw from the `metrics` snapshots; a frozen scope's countdown runs on the existing 1 s clock. `/v1/admin/limits/{scope}` is read only when its fold opens, and history once per Cache entry.
- **Phones.** Under 820 px the health strip no longer sticks under the tab strip (together they took 150 px of the screen); under 480 px meters narrow their label and number columns.
- **Tests:** `tests/admin_routes.rs` (a dry run counts and leaves the entry cached; a non-boolean `dryRun` is refused); `tests/dom/admin-limiter.test.mjs` (order, summary, badge, meters' role, values, text and icons, methods fullest first and folded, a held method, the countdown without a refetch, live usage on expand, folds kept across snapshots); `tests/dom/admin-cache.test.mjs` (tiles, the byte meter, the hit-ratio chart, the probe and its error, count then confirm then report, a declined purge, disarming on edit); `tests/dom/admin.browser.test.mjs` (both tabs, folds open, at three widths in two themes); `tests/admin_ui.mjs` (window labels, fullness, meter levels, scope order).

## ADR-155 — The dashboard's Jobs tab folds in /dev/jobs (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-11). design/13 §Jobs; design/10 §Jobs page.
- **Why.** `/dev/jobs` is the only live view of the workers, and `/dev*` is never served in production, where the stale crawl backlog had to be purged by hand (DSK-03). The dashboard is served there behind Access and the admin key.
- **What.** The Jobs tab ports `/dev/jobs`'s worker cards, finished list, failed list and per-job trace with `?after=` follow, and the v1 dashboard's grouped up-next list (ADR-093), recovered from history. Queue totals come from the snapshot rather than a fetch. Each part polls at its own pace on one 1 s chain: workers every tick, the queue and failures every 5th, counts by kind every 15th; nothing runs while the tab is hidden, left, or not *live*.
- **Bulk purge.** The DSK-03 route gets a form: Delete is only enabled by a dry run of the same kind, state and minimum priority, and the confirm names the matched count. This is what the owner ran by hand on 2026-10-13 to drop 1.13 M stale crawl jobs.
- **Writes.** Retry (failed) and Cancel (pending) go through `confirmAction()` and a toast reports the outcome.
- **Tests:** `tests/dom/admin-jobs.test.mjs` (eight cases, the four ported from `jobs.test.mjs` under their names: overview, a worker opening a trace followed with `after=`, a link opening a job, a missing job; plus ADR-093 grouping, retry and cancel with confirm, the purge's dry-run gate, polling only while live and visible); `tests/dom/admin.browser.test.mjs` (the tab and a trace at three widths, two themes); `tests/admin_ui.mjs` (durations, times, job labels, grouping).

## ADR-156 — The Ladder tab returns in the new dashboard (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-12). design/13 §Ladder. Carries ADR-087 and ADR-091 over from v1's page.
- **What.** The crawl cards (stage progress bars, totals, the apex-cap note, folded details), the past-crawls list paged ten at a time with a row opening into its detail, the start form from `/v1/admin/ladder/options` and the analytics recompute fold, ported from the page ADM-08 deleted (recovered from history) into layout A's tokens and shared helpers. An opened run is now a link, `#ladder/<id>`.
- **Changes from v1's page.** The job-queue panel stays in the Jobs tab (ADM-11) instead of being repeated here. Start, Cancel and Recompute go through `confirmAction()` like every other write. Statuses carry an icon and words. Under 1000 px the history drops four columns so an opened run fits the screen. The start form is opened only once the page knows nothing runs (v1's opened it before its data arrived).
- **Refresh.** One 5 s `setTimeout` chain while the tab is open and visible: running crawls' activity, the crawl list every sixth tick, the options until they load; a `crawl.phase` or `ladder.crawl.completed` frame refreshes at once. A finished run's activity is read once. Nothing runs elsewhere.
- **Tests:** `tests/dom/admin-ladder.test.mjs` (the eight cases of v1's `dashboard.test.mjs` under their names, its ninth being the Jobs tab's now; starting a crawl with a confirm; no refresh off the tab); `tests/dashboard.rs` (`the_dashboard_follows_crawl_phases` back, against `admin.html`); `tests/ui.rs` (`the_pages_apex_cap_is_the_servers`, unchanged); `tests/dom/admin.browser.test.mjs` (the tab and an opened run at three widths, two themes, every part of the opened run on screen); `tests/admin_ui.mjs` (stage text, progress, legs, tiers).

## ADR-157 — Archive & Storage tab (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-13). design/13 §Archive & Storage.
- **Why.** The 97 % disk of 2026-10-13 (DSK) was found by a host monitor, not by the dashboard; nothing showed the file, its free pages, the WAL or whether backups were being written.
