- **What.** One tab for what the archive costs and whether it is safe: archive and storage tiles; the data disk as a stacked bar (live pages, free pages, WAL, other files, free), every part named in a legend so colour carries no meaning alone; warnings at 85 % and 95 % used, here and in the health strip; the backups with a newest one older than 36 h flagged and the last maintenance run reported (ADM-06); the timeline backfill's progress (TL-02); the analytics runs; and the actions that rebuild derived data (recompute, re-extract, Data Dragon sync, names backfill) and run maintenance, each behind `confirmAction()`.
- **No new routes.** Everything comes from `/v1/admin/stats`, `/backups`, `/timelines/backfill`, the snapshot, and the existing POST routes. The tab refreshes on entry and every 60 s while open and visible, matching how often the bytes are sampled (ADM-02).
- **Thresholds.** 85 % and 95 % are design/13's; 36 h is one missed nightly run plus slack, as ADM-13's spec set.
- **Tests:** `tests/dom/admin-archive.test.mjs` (tiles; the bar's parts in its label and legend; the under-5 % warning with an icon here and in the strip; a stale backup flagged; a fresh one not; keep 0; the last run reported; run-now after a confirm; the timeline meter and regions; the four actions after confirms, a declined one posting nothing); `tests/dom/admin.browser.test.mjs` (the tab at three widths, two themes); `tests/admin_ui.mjs` (the storage split adds up to the disk).

## ADR-158 — Consumers & Players tab; tracked players filter (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-14). design/13 §Consumers & Players.
- **What.** Consumers with their quota use this minute (ADM-04's `quotas`), create, revoke and re-check; tracked players with track and untrack. Every write goes through `confirmAction()`.
- **A new key is shown once.** The page puts the plaintext key from `POST /v1/admin/consumers` only into a warning box with Copy and Done, and nowhere else: not the store, `localStorage`, a toast or the console. Done removes it. This matches the route: only the SHA-256 is stored server-side.
- **Re-check by hash.** `revoke-cache` takes the key's SHA-256, which the page cannot know for another consumer. The operator pastes the key (hashed in the page with `crypto.subtle`, so the key itself is never sent) or the hash.
- **`GET /v1/admin/tracked-players?tracked=true`.** Additive: without it the route is unchanged; with it, the tracked rows only (`players::list_tracked`). The unfiltered list is every player a crawl discovered (41 K on production), too much for a table the page reloads.
- **Revoking the page's own key** needs no special case: the next call is a 401 and the key sheet opens (ADM-08).
- **Space.** `admin.html`'s style and script lose their last two spaces of base indentation (4 KB), leaving room under the 160 KB budget for ADM-15 and ADM-16.
- **Tests:** `tests/admin_routes.rs` (`tracked=true` lists tracked rows and leaves an untracked one out; a bad value is refused); `tests/dom/admin-consumers.test.mjs` (the list, quota meter and revoked rows; create showing the key once, copying it, and keeping it out of storage, toasts and the store; revoke after a confirm, and revoking the page's own key opening the key sheet; re-check hashing a key in the page or sending a pasted hash; tracking by Riot ID and PUUID, untracking); `tests/dom/admin.browser.test.mjs` (the tab at three widths, two themes); `tests/admin_ui.mjs` (Riot ID parsing, hash recognition); the OpenAPI snapshot.

## ADR-159 — Events and About tabs (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-15). design/13 §Events and About.
- **Events.** The firehose is kept in the page as a 2,000-frame ring regardless of the tab, so opening Events shows what happened before. The list is drawn in 250 ms batches and never while paused, so a burst (a crawl archiving thousands of matches) costs one redraw per batch, not one per frame, and a paused list is not touched at all. `metrics.snapshot` is filtered out by default: it is every 5 s and is the page's own feed. The names come from one list in the page that a test checks against `src/events.rs`, so a new event cannot be missing from the filters.
- **About.** Renders `/v1/admin/info` (ADM-03) and `/readyz` with no new route. The configuration is searchable; the page adds no redaction of its own and relies on the server's.
- **Tests:** `tests/dom/admin-events.test.mjs` (10,000 frames keep 2,000 newest first; snapshots hidden by default; name filters, counts and search; pause with no list mutations, the count, and resume; a frame's JSON staying open; the unseen badge); `tests/dom/admin-about.test.mjs` (build, uptime, features, readiness, links; the configuration listed, searched, redacted); `tests/dom/admin.browser.test.mjs` (both tabs, a frame opened, three widths, two themes); `tests/admin_ui.mjs` (the ring and filter, and the event names matching `src/events.rs`).

## ADR-160 — Dashboard keyboard, command palette and accessibility checks (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-16). design/13 §Keyboard.
- **What.** A command palette on `/` (a `combobox` over a `listbox`, `aria-activedescendant` for the chosen row) listing the tabs and actions and offering a pasted id as a job or a crawl; `?` for the keys; `1`–`9` and `0` for the tabs; `t` for the theme; Esc to close. Tab is kept inside an open dialog (key sheet, confirm, palette, help) and focus returns to where it was when one closes.
- **Not while typing.** Shortcuts fire only with no field focused, no modifier and no dialog open, so `/` in a search box is a slash and Ctrl+1 is the browser's.
- **Ids.** The palette accepts any 26 letters and digits as an id rather than Crockford's alphabet only: the cost of a looser match is two extra suggestions, and test fixtures and hand-typed ids pass.
- **Names.** Columns without a label (row actions) now head with a visually hidden "Actions" instead of an empty sort button; `.scroll` became a containing block so that hidden text cannot widen a phone screen.
- **Size.** The page is about 154 KB of its 160 KB budget (`tests/ui.rs`); ADM-17 removes nothing from it, and further growth needs the budget raised by the owner.
- **Tests:** `tests/dom/admin-keyboard.test.mjs` (palette filtering, arrows, Enter, focus return; an id opening a job; an action and Esc; help, digits, theme, keys ignored in a field and with a modifier; Tab trapped both ways and shortcuts held while a dialog is open; every interactive element on every tab named); `tests/dom/admin.browser.test.mjs` (the same name audit and tab-to-panel check on every view, and the palette on screen, at three widths in two themes); `tests/admin_ui.mjs` (palette matching and digit keys).

## ADR-161 — The old dashboard and jobs paths are retired (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task ADM-17). design/13 (now Accepted), design/10 §Jobs page.
- **What.** `/dashboard/config.json` is removed: the page probes `/v1/admin/info` instead (ADM-08), so nothing reads it. `/dev/jobs` and its file are removed, and the address answers `302` to `/dashboard#jobs` wherever `/dev*` is served (never in production, ADR-071), so bookmarks land on the Jobs tab (ADM-11). The page bar drops its Jobs link. Its jsdom suite and fake go with it; their cases live on in `tests/dom/admin-jobs.test.mjs`.
- **Docs.** design/03, 06, 07, 10 and 12 now point at the dashboard instead; design/13 is Accepted. Finished task specs in IMPLEMENTATION.md keep the file names they were written with, as a record of what each task touched; the grep in ADM-17's spec is applied to `src`, `tests` and `docs/design`.
- **Size.** The binary loses the old jobs page (about 25 KB of embedded HTML); `admin.html` is unchanged.
- **Tests:** `tests/ui.rs` (`/dev/jobs` answers 302 to `/dashboard#jobs` and leaves the bar; with `DASHBOARD_UI=false` or outside development it is a 404; `/dashboard/config.json` is a 404; no page links to either; the bar's link list without Jobs).

## ADR-162 — The object store client and its configuration (2026-10-13)
Accepted (task OBJ-01). design/12 §Read and write paths, §Configuration; ADR-146.
- **What.** `archive::objects::Objects` wraps `object_store` 0.14 (`aws` feature, plus its default `fs`) with the six calls packs need: `put(key, bytes, metadata)`, `head`, `get_range`, `get_ranges`, `list(prefix)` and `delete`. Nothing calls it yet; OBJ-02 reads through it and OBJ-03 writes.
- **Uploads.** A body up to 8 MiB is one PUT; above that, a multipart upload of 8 MiB parts (the last shorter), four in flight. Parts are equal because R2 requires it and S3 wants at least 5 MiB. A failed part aborts the upload, so no half-written object or dangling upload is left by the proxy (R2's one-day multipart rule in OBJ-04 is the backstop). Every object gets `Content-Type: application/octet-stream` and the caller's metadata as `x-amz-meta-*`.
- **Reads are whole or fail.** HTTP clamps a range that runs past the end of an object; `get_range` and `get_ranges` turn any short answer into `ShortRead`, so a truncated body never reaches the sha256 check as if it were complete. `head` and `delete` treat a missing key as `None` and success.
- **Keys** are checked before any request: no leading, trailing or doubled `/`, no `.` or `..` segment.
- **Back ends.** `s3://<bucket>` is the production store; `file:///<dir>` (development and the OBJ-03 tests) cannot hold metadata, so `put` drops it and `head` returns none; `InMemory` for unit tests.
- **Boot.** With a URL set, `serve` builds the client (no request) and logs `object store configured` with the bucket and endpoint; a client that cannot be built stops the boot.
- **Addressing.** Onidel's knowledge base documents virtual-hosted addressing (`<bucket>.s3.ap-southeast-2.onidel.cloud`) and says nothing of path style; R2 accepts path style. Rather than a new variable, the endpoint decides: a host that starts with `<bucket>.` is used as it is (virtual-hosted), any other gets `/<bucket>` appended (path style). The owner confirms on the box which form Onidel accepts before the first deploy with a URL. *Verified 2026-10-14 (owner, signed with region `ap-southeast-2`): list, PUT, GET, ranged GET (206) and DELETE all work in both forms, so the default path-style endpoint stands. `auto` was not tried; production sets `ARCHIVE_OBJECT_REGION=ap-southeast-2`.*
- **TLS.** The client trusts only the bundled Mozilla roots (`webpki-root-certs`), as the Riot client does; a static binary never depends on the host's store.
- **Config.** `ARCHIVE_OBJECT_URL`, `_ENDPOINT` (required with `s3://`, trailing `/` dropped), `_REGION` (`auto`), `_ACCESS_KEY` / `_SECRET_KEY` (both or neither, held as `Secret`, `[REDACTED]` in `Config::redacted()`) and `_CONCURRENCY` (16, 1–256). Production refuses an `s3://` URL without both keys, a `file://` URL and an `http://` endpoint. Keys without a URL are accepted and inert, so they can go into `/etc/riot-proxy/env` before the bucket exists. The other design/12 variables (`ARCHIVE_BODY_LOCAL_DAYS`, `ARCHIVE_PACK_TARGET_BYTES`, `ARCHIVE_OFFLOAD_PACE_MS`, `ARCHIVE_COMPACT_BELOW`) arrive with the tasks that read them.
- **Size: the cap is raised to 24 MiB (owner, 2026-10-14).** `object_store` shares reqwest 0.13, rustls and aws-lc-rs with the proxy; what it adds is its S3 client, XML parsing, chrono and CRC code. In this PR only `serve`'s boot log builds a client, so LTO drops the request paths and the static musl binary barely moves (19,118,016 bytes on main's CI, about 19.08 MB locally). A local probe that calls all six methods measured 20,871,760 bytes: about 1.75 MB more, about 97 KiB under the old 20 MiB cap and inside the roughly 70 KB local-to-CI drift, before OBJ-02/03's own code. Dependencies at `opt-level = "z"` saved only 28 KB. The owner raised the cap rather than replace the client: the binary should stay lightweight, with dependencies added only when needed and the code kept robust, and a hard number matters less than that. `build-musl` in `ci.yml` and the release job in `release.yml` now check `< 24 * 1024 * 1024`. ADR-061's `opt-level = "s"` for dependencies stays.
- **Tests:** `src/archive/objects/tests.rs` (over `InMemory`: put then ranged get; several ranges in order; a range past the end is `ShortRead`; head gives size, time and metadata, `None` when missing; up to 8 MiB is one PUT, above it equal 8 MiB parts with the metadata kept; a failed part aborts and leaves no object; list by prefix in key order; delete is idempotent; malformed keys refused; a `file://` directory round-trips and drops metadata; an S3 store builds without a request and never prints its keys; virtual-hosted detection); `tests/cli.rs` (the boot line names bucket and endpoint, never the keys); `src/config/tests.rs` (defaults; URL forms; every bad value reported; production's three refusals; keys redacted in `Debug` and `redacted()`).

## ADR-163 — Pack format, body locations and reads by range (2026-10-14)
Accepted (task OBJ-02). design/12 §Pack format, §SQLite, §Read and write paths; ADR-146, ADR-162.
- **Format (`archive::pack`).** Bodies of one kind, sorted by match id as text (SQLite's `ORDER BY match_id`), back to back from offset 0, each the stored zstd frame byte for byte; then the zstd-compressed JSON-lines index (`{"kind","matchId","offset","len","sha256"}`, sha256 in hex), its length as u64 LE, and `RPXPACK1`. Building refuses no bodies, a repeated id and an empty body. Reading refuses a missing or wrong trailer, an index longer than the object, an entry of the other kind, outside the bodies, out of id order or overlapping. Keys are `packs/<matches|timelines>/<yyyy>/<mm>/<ULID>.pack`, year and month (UTC) from the ULID. Object metadata: `sha256` of the whole object and `bodies`.
- **Schema (V0023).** As design/12, with two changes. `body_packs.kind` is added: the key needs it, and a pack whose last body is deleted (OBJ-06) would otherwise lose it. Each partial index ends with its state columns (`matches_to_pack (archived_at, packed_at)`, `…_to_free (…, packed_at, offloaded_at)`, the same on `timelines`). SQLite used the partial indexes without them, but not as covering indexes: it checked each term against the row, and in a row the added columns sit after the body, so a count would read every body. A test pins the four query plans to `USING COVERING INDEX`. Existing rows are untouched: their records end before the new columns, which read as NULL, so every existing body is local after the migration.
- **Migration cost on production.** The `matches_*` indexes are keyed on `archived_at`, which sits after the body in each row, so building them reads every match body once (about 2.5 GB on production) while V0023 runs at boot. The timeline indexes are keyed on `match_id` and read only the start of each row. Expect a slower start than V0022, a few minutes at most on Onidel's disk.
- **Where the bucket lives.** `Db::attach_objects` attaches `Objects` to the database handle once, at boot (`serve`, when `ARCHIVE_OBJECT_URL` is set). Offloaded bodies are only readable through that database's locations, so the handle is the natural owner, and the five callers of `get`/`get_many`/`get_timeline(s)` outside `src/archive` keep working unchanged. `ARCHIVE_OBJECT_CONCURRENCY` travels on `Objects`.
- **Reads.** One query per chunk reads each row's blob with a `LEFT JOIN` on its location. A non-empty blob is decompressed as before, so packed bodies inside the window are local reads. An empty blob (`X''`, never a body) is fetched: ids are grouped by pack, each pack is one `get_ranges` call, packs run at the configured concurrency, every body is checked against its sha256 and decompressed off the runtime. A missing location, no store attached, a failed pack read, a digest mismatch or a bad frame drops that id with a warning (one per pack for a failed read), so the fetcher goes to Riot. Bodies read from the bucket are not written back.
- **Writes.** Unchanged, except that archiving a match or timeline again clears `offloaded_at`: a body fetched from Riot after a failed bucket read is local again, and its location, still valid because bodies are immutable, stays.
- **`archive::offload`.** The two steps OBJ-03's job will drive. `write_pack(db, objects, kind, ids, now)` reads the ids' local bodies (freed ones skipped), builds the pack off the runtime, uploads it, HEADs it (size and `sha256` metadata; a store without metadata is read back and hashed), then records it in one transaction (`pack::record_on`: `body_packs`, a location per body, `packed_at`; a body already located elsewhere moves and its old pack's `live_bytes` drops). `free(db, kind, ids, now)` sets `X''` and `offloaded_at` only on rows that are packed, not yet freed and have a location. Choosing which ids, and when, is OBJ-03's.
- **Stats.** `matches::Stats` gains each kind's local, packed and offloaded counts (from the partial indexes; offloaded is the rest), and the pack count, bytes and live bytes. `/v1/admin/stats` adds `archiveBodies {matches, timelines}` and `archivePacks`, `archivePackBytes`, `archivePackLiveBytes`; `archiveStoredBytes` now counts only bodies still in SQLite. The dashboard's Archive tab adds an Object storage tile, and its Stored tile drops the zstd ratio once anything is offloaded, since the ratio no longer compares like with like.
- **`/dev/reset`** wipes `body_locations` and `body_packs` with the archive. Their objects stay in the bucket as orphans for OBJ-06's sweep; dev VMs use a local-directory store.
- **Tests:** `src/archive/pack/tests.rs` (round trip; key and metadata; the index's JSON lines; bad inputs; truncated, wrong-magic, oversized-index and wrong-kind packs; a damaged index; a changed body fails verification; recording from the index alone rebuilds every row; a re-packed body moves and lowers the old pack's live bytes); `src/archive/offload/tests.rs` (an offloaded match reads byte-identical; a packed body keeps its local copy; `get_many` mixes local, packed and offloaded ids over several packs; timelines; a missing location, a store error, a digest mismatch and no store each return nothing and warn; only packed, located rows are freed; `write_pack` skips freed bodies; the uploaded object's metadata; a local-directory store; archiving again makes a body local; the state counts and their query plans); `src/db/tests.rs` (V0023 on a file seeded at V0022); `tests/archive.rs` (an offloaded match and timeline answer `X-Cache: ARCHIVE` with the same body and headers and no Riot call; a lost pack falls back to Riot and is local again); `tests/players_routes.rs` (a page over matches offloaded into two packs is byte-identical); `tests/admin_routes.rs`, the OpenAPI snapshot, `tests/dom/admin-archive.test.mjs`.

## ADR-164 — The offload job (2026-10-14)
Accepted (task OBJ-03). design/12 §Offload job; ADR-146, ADR-163.
- **What.** `archive:offload` (`jobs::offload`), priority 30 000, no lane, registered only when `ARCHIVE_OBJECT_URL` is set (unset is the rollback). A run packs, matches first, while a pack is due: a full one (`ARCHIVE_PACK_TARGET_BYTES`, 64 MiB), or a partial one when any of its bodies is past the window. It then frees, 2,000 rows a transaction, the packed and located bodies whose match was archived more than `ARCHIVE_BODY_LOCAL_DAYS` (14) ago. Finally it re-reads what is left for the trigger and the gauges. It pauses `ARCHIVE_OFFLOAD_PACE_MS` (50) between turns. Packing and freeing are OBJ-02's `archive::offload::{write_pack, free}`.
- **When.** Three triggers. (1) `Pending` counts the zstd bytes of each stored match and timeline (`put_stored` / `put_timeline_stored`, so `put`'s callers are unchanged), and the first time a kind reaches a full pack it queues the job, deduplicated, at most once per run. (2) `serve` queues the job at boot, which covers a counter lost to a restart. (3) `maintenance` queues it after each daily run, whatever that run did, so bodies leave the window daily and a partial pack whose bodies are past the window gets written.
- **Timelines have no archive time.** A timeline's window runs from its match's `archived_at`; one whose match isn't archived yet counts as new and is kept. Timelines are packed in match-id order, the order of their "to pack" index, rather than by their match's age: ordering by age would read every unpacked timeline each turn, about 220 k rows a turn on the first run.
- **V0024.** `matches_archived ON matches(match_id, archived_at)`. A match's `archived_at` sits after its body, so finding the timelines to free would read a match row, overflow pages and all, per timeline: about 2.5 GB on the first run and about 300 MB a day after. The index makes those joins covering (a test pins both plans) for about 40 bytes a match. Building it reads `archived_at` once, like V0023's indexes. SQLite also prefers it over the key's own index for the rank lookup's `match_id` range, an equivalent plan; that test now accepts either.
- **Failure.** A store or database error fails the run with `Retry`, so the scheduler's backoff applies. Nothing is marked packed unless verified, and nothing is freed unless packed and located. A crash after an upload leaves an orphan object, and the next run packs the rows into a new pack; OBJ-06 sweeps orphans. A run aborted at shutdown is re-queued at boot.
- **One at a time.** `JOB_KIND_LIMITS` now defaults to `aggregate:analytics=1,archive:offload=1`. The dedupe already covers a running job; the cap also covers an owner who queues one by hand.
- **Metrics.** `archive_offload_pending{kind,step="pack"|"free"}` and `archive_packs` are set at the end of each run; `archive_offload_bytes_total{kind}` counts uploaded pack bytes.
- **Not in this task.** No admin route to queue the job by hand; restarting or waiting for maintenance covers it until OBJ-04.
- **Amended 2026-10-14 (trigger fix).** On production the first run took 32 minutes. During it, a kind reached a full pack, and the trigger's request was absorbed by the dedupe because the run was still going. The trigger stayed latched anyway, so no later full pack queued a run (23 k matches waited until the next boot or maintenance). The latch is now set only when the queue creates a job. An absorbed request leaves it clear and is repeated at most once a minute (`RETRY_ABSORBED`), so the first body stored after a run ends queues the next one. Test: `a_trigger_absorbed_by_a_running_job_tries_again_after_it_ends`.
- **Tests:** `src/jobs/offload/tests.rs` (paused time: a full pack queues one job, less queues none, a second full pack while one is pending queues no duplicate; a run resets the counter from the database; new bodies are packed once a pack is full, inside the window, each pack one kind in match-id order, local copies kept and still read locally; less than a pack waits unless a body is past the window; only packed bodies past the window are freed, and with a zero window all of them; a timeline goes by its match's age and one with no match stays; a failed upload marks nothing and fails the job; a crash after the upload leaves an orphan and the next run packs again; the backlog and counter after a run; maintenance queues the job only with a bucket; the free lookups' plans); `tests/offload.rs` (bodies stored through `SqliteArchive` queue the job; 500 matches with timelines offload into several packs in a local-directory bucket and all read back); `tests/cli.rs` (`serve` with a `file://` bucket queues and runs the job at boot; without one it doesn't); `src/db/tests.rs`, `src/config/tests.rs`, `metrics.md`.

## ADR-165 — Index snapshots, reindex, restore and the bucket replica (2026-10-14)
Accepted (task OBJ-04). design/12 §Replica and backups; ADR-146, ADR-163, ADR-164.
- **Snapshots (`archive::recovery`, `archive:index-snapshot`).** Once a day, after `maintenance` (which now queues it beside `archive:offload`; deduped, priority 30 000, no lane, registered only with `ARCHIVE_OBJECT_URL`), one read transaction streams `body_packs` and `body_locations` as JSON lines into two zstd objects, `index/packs-<date>.jsonl.zst` and `index/locations-<date>.jsonl.zst` (UTC date; the locations first). Each line is a table row with its digest in hex. Each object is one frame with zstd's content checksum, so a damaged byte fails the decode rather than parsing as something else. Metadata: `last-pack` (design/12) and `rows`. A second run the same day replaces the pair. `riot-proxy packs snapshot` writes one on demand. Snapshots are deleted by the bucket's lifecycle rule (14 days on Onidel), not by the job.
- **Reindex.** `riot-proxy packs reindex [--since <ULID>]` lists `packs/`, keeps keys that parse back to themselves (`pack::parse_key`), and reads them oldest first, `ARCHIVE_OBJECT_CONCURRENCY` at a time: a HEAD for the object's digest, then the trailer and the index. With no `sha256` metadata (a local directory) the object is read whole and hashed. Each pack is recorded in its own transaction: its `body_packs` row if missing (`created_at` from the ULID, which is what the offload stores), then `pack::locate_on` (split out of `record_on`), so a body in two packs ends in the newer, its old pack's live bytes drop, and the pack's own live bytes are recounted from its locations. Without the recount, a full reindex after a partial one left a pre-existing row at zero (a test caught it). A pack that cannot be read is reported and the rest are recorded; the command exits non-zero. Re-running is safe.
- **Restore.** `riot-proxy packs restore-index` lists `index/`, takes dates with both objects, newest first, and loads the first pair that decodes, parses and agrees: every pack id a ULID and listed once, every digest 32 bytes, every location in a listed pack of its kind, and each pack's `live_bytes` equal to the lengths located in it. That last check is what catches a locations object cut to an empty frame, which decodes and parses. The offload keeps the invariant; were it ever broken, every snapshot would be passed over and the restore would reindex every pack, slower but still right. It replaces both tables in one transaction, sets `packed_at` (to the pack's creation) on located rows that lack it, then reindexes `--since` the snapshot's newest pack id, read from the snapshot's own rows because a local directory keeps no metadata. A snapshot that fails is skipped with its reason. With none left, every pack is reindexed. Run it with the service stopped.
- **Not reconstructed.** Archive rows themselves (`matches`, `timelines`) are not rebuilt from packs: the map points at bodies whose rows come from the database or its backup. A location for a body whose row is gone is harmless and unread.
- **Replica.** `deploy/riot-proxy-replica.service` runs `rclone sync onidel:riot-proxy-archive r2:riot-proxy-archive --metadata --transfers 16` as a `DynamicUser`. `--metadata` carries each pack's `x-amz-meta-sha256` to R2; without it (as first installed on production, 2026-10-14), a reindex against the replica must read and hash every pack whole. `/etc/riot-proxy/rclone.conf` is passed as a systemd credential (`LoadCredential`, `%d`) so the sync reads no other file. The timer runs at :17 every hour, with `Persistent=true`. `deploy/README.md` has the remotes (`no_check_bucket` for bucket-scoped tokens, R2's `no_head` from the move), the lifecycle rules (Onidel `index/` 14 days; R2 `index/` 21 days and multipart abort 1 day; nothing on `packs/`), recovery, the quarterly drill (against R2 with a read-only token, so the drill's boot offload cannot write into the replica) and the reclaim after the offload. design/07 §Reclaiming space already had the `VACUUM INTO` swap; it now points there.
- **CI.** The `ops` job installs `rclone` and runs `deploy/test.sh`: shellcheck, `systemd-analyze verify` on both units, and the unit's own `ExecStart` (remotes swapped for two local directories) with `--dry-run` (nothing changes; the new pack and snapshot would be copied, the expired one deleted, the shared pack left alone), then for real (`diff -r` clean).
- **Tests:** `src/archive/recovery/tests.rs`, over a local directory that records every key read (a snapshot round-trips both tables exactly; its metadata; `reindex` rebuilds an emptied `body_locations`, and an emptied `body_packs`, exactly and is idempotent; `restore-index` from a snapshot plus two newer packs is exact and reads only those two packs; a corrupt newest snapshot, one whose halves disagree and one whose locations are an empty frame fall back to the day before; half a snapshot doesn't count and every pack is reindexed; an unreadable pack is reported and the rest recorded; `--since` reads only newer packs and the newest pack keeps a body both hold; a restore marks located bodies packed; pack keys parse back); `src/jobs/offload/tests.rs` (maintenance queues the snapshot too, only with a bucket); `tests/cli.rs` (`packs snapshot`, `restore-index`, `reindex` and `--since` end to end; no bucket, no command; every `packs` subcommand has help); `deploy/test.sh`.

## ADR-166 — Analytics reads: seek, sum over tier once, cache per generation (2026-10-14)
Accepted (task PERF-00). design/04 §Analytics reads; ADR-099, ADR-102, ADR-123.
- **Why.** ninjagoldfinch.lol's `/champions/__data.json` took 3.9 s on production. The site's three proxy calls already run in parallel and its payload is about 8 kB on the wire. The critical call is `GET /v1/lol/analytics/champions?queue=RANKED_SOLO_5x5&patch=all&minGames=0&limit=500`. It runs `stat_rows` and the four rate reads on every request. The `ETag` saves nothing, because it hashes `computed_at` from that same SQL, and the site revalidates every call (ADR-099).
- **Measured.** A synthetic database with 4 platforms, 2 queues, 6 tiers, 8 patches, 171 champions and 1–5 roles per champion: `champion_stats` 375,608 rows, `champion_bans` 65,664. Python's SQLite 3.46, mean of 3 warm runs, on this container. Production's analytics tables are about 475 MB (DSK series), so several times larger.

| read | site slice, no stats | site slice, `ANALYZE` | oc1 + one patch, no stats | oc1 + one patch, `ANALYZE` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `stat_rows` today | 228 ms | 710 ms | 48 ms | 504 ms |
| `patches` today | 108 ms | 566 ms | 55 ms | 516 ms |
| `latest_patch` today | 45 ms | 44 ms | 37 ms | 41 ms |
| `bans` today | 21 ms | 22 ms | 8 ms | 7 ms |
| `stat_rows`, seeking `IN` lists | 214 ms | 211 ms | 6 ms | 14 ms |
| `stat_rows`, tier-summed table | 32 ms | — | 0.8 ms | — |

  Every read today seeks on `key_scope` alone (`SEARCH … USING PRIMARY KEY (key_scope=?)`), then sorts with two temp B-trees. With statistics (`PRAGMA optimize` runs at open and before each rebuild, ADR-102), `stat_rows` and `patches` switch to `champion_stats_slice`. That index doesn't cover the summed columns, so each row costs a second lookup. Seeking `IN` lists fix the one-platform reads (504 → 14 ms) and the statistics regression. But the site's slice spans every platform, patch and tier, so it still sums the whole queue. Summing over tier at rebuild time cuts that read to a sixth (138,102 rows, +1.7 s on this rebuild).
- **Decision.** Three steps. PERF-01: every read seeks, filling open filters from the slice's own values. PERF-02: `champion_stats_totals`, summed over tier, for tierless reads. PERF-03: an in-memory response cache per aggregate generation, warmed after each rebuild. This reverses "the analytics routes revalidate with `ETag` instead of a cache tier" (`routes/docs.rs`): the data only changes on a rebuild, so a cache cleared on `analytics.updated` can't serve stale numbers.
- **Rejected.** A response cache alone: a cold or unwarmed slice (a role, a tier, one platform) would still take seconds on production. Summing over platform as well: the one-platform reads are already under a millisecond, and the all-platform slice is cached. A covering `champion_stats_slice`: as wide as the table; PERF-02 drops the index instead.
- **Latency on production** is read from the dashboard's Traffic tab (p50/p95 by route, ADM-05). No new metric.

## ADR-167 — Analytics reads seek on their keys (2026-10-14)
Accepted (task PERF-01). design/04 §Analytics reads; ADR-166.
- **What.** `archive::analytics::Filter` builds every analytics read's `WHERE` and its arguments, replacing `read_where`. A filter that is set is an equality. A filter left open (no platform, no tier, `patch=all`) becomes an `IN` over the values the slice has, so the key is followed through `patch`, and to the champion on the facet and build tables. An open tier is an `IN` only where the key has tier before patch (slices, stats, bans). On the facet tables tier comes after the champion, so it is simply left out. `remake = 0` unless remakes are asked for, and a side is still written as one of two constants. There is no `(? IS NULL OR col = ?)` left in the reads.
- **Where the open values come from.** `SELECT DISTINCT platform, tier, patch FROM analytics_slices` for the key scope and queue (and platform, if named), at the start of each read. It is a few hundred rows. Every row of the other tables comes from a fact whose match the same rebuild counts in a slice at that platform, tier and patch, and `rebuild_champions` writes the slices before matchups and builds. The values are not taken from config, so a platform dropped from `LADDER_PLATFORMS` still counts, as before. `latest_patch` is now the newest of those patches, compared as numbers. It no longer runs a `DISTINCT` over `champion_stats`.
- **Measured** (the ADR-166 synthetic copy; the generated SQL run as-is). The site's slice, with statistics: 710 → 208 ms, `SEARCH champion_stats USING PRIMARY KEY (key_scope=? AND platform=? AND queue=? AND tier=? AND patch=?)`. One platform and one patch: 504 → 14 ms. The site's slice still sums its whole queue; PERF-02 addresses that.
- **Results unchanged.** Every existing read test passes as it was. `tests/dashboard.rs` now writes the slices its stats rows imply, as a rebuild does. A slice with no rows reads `IN ()`, which matches nothing, as before.
- **Tests.** `every_read_seeks_on_its_key` checks all eleven tables, with every filter open and with every filter set, with and without `ANALYZE`. With the fixture's statistics SQLite may stop at `tier` on the two smallest tables, so the `ANALYZE` case only requires the `platform, queue` seek. `an_open_filter_is_the_slices_values`, `the_newest_patch_compares_numbers`.

## ADR-168 — Tierless stats reads sum a tier-summed table (2026-10-15)
Accepted (task PERF-02). design/04 §Schema, §Analytics reads; ADR-123, ADR-166, ADR-167.
- **What.** V0025 creates `champion_stats_totals`, `champion_stats` summed over tier, keyed `(key_scope, platform, queue, patch, champion_id, role, remake, side)`, `WITHOUT ROWID` (ADR-140), with the same value columns and `computed_at` the newest tier's. The migration fills it from `champion_stats` in key order (V0017's pattern). `rebuild_champions` clears it with the other tables and refills it in the same transaction, summing the `champion_stats` rows it has just written for the same key scope, ladder and patches, so it can't drift from them and a partial rebuild (ADR-094) leaves other patches' rows alone. V0025 also drops `champion_stats_slice`: every read seeks on its key since PERF-01, and with statistics the planner chose the non-covering index for a full read (ADR-166).
- **Which reads.** One helper, `stats_source`, picks the table: the totals when the read names no tier and wants no row per tier. That is `stats` without `tier` (`/champions`, a champion's summed row, the dashboard's top champions), `patches`, and `top_role`. `stats_by_tier` (`byTier`) and any read with `tier` stay on `champion_stats`. The totals have no tier column, so the helper is the only way to reach them. Their `Keys` has `TierKey::None`, so `Filter` writes no tier term and the key is followed through `patch`, and to the champion when one is named.
- **A two-step sum for the summed stats.** Summing 13 columns over about 69,000 totals rows, `GROUP BY champion_id` still sorted every row in a temp B-tree: 64–68 ms. That missed PERF-02's 50 ms (ADR-166's 32 ms was measured summing four columns). `stat_rows` without a tier now sums per `(platform, patch, champion_id)` first, a run that follows the key order and needs no sort. It then sums those few thousand rows per champion (owner's choice, 2026-10-15). The integers are the same, so the answer is the same. The one-platform, one-patch read pays about 0.5 ms for it.
- **Results unchanged.** Before, the tierless read wrote `tier IN (…)` over every tier in the slice. Every `champion_stats` row's tier is in its slice (ADR-167), so that term already took every tier, and the totals sum the same rows. `matches_picked` stays a plain sum over tiers, as the read computed it. A champion is picked at most once per match, so the sum counts each match once. In any case the pick rate's denominator comes from `analytics_match_totals` (ADR-123). Every existing result test passes as it was. `Written` (the `analytics.updated` payload and the dashboard's run record) still reports `champion_stats` alone. The totals' count is derived from it and would only change the payload.
- **Measured** on the ADR-166 synthetic copy: 375,608 `champion_stats` rows, regenerated by `scripts/perf-synthetic.py` (seed 1), which also runs the before and after SQL as the code writes it. "Cold" is a fresh connection's first read, the domain query included; "warm" is the mean of three more.

| read | SQLite 3.46, no stats | 3.46, `ANALYZE` | SQLite 3.51 (as bundled), no stats | 3.51, `ANALYZE` |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| site slice `stat_rows`, before (cold / warm) | 219 / 207 ms | 226 / 209 ms | 182 / 180 ms | 184 / 186 ms |
| site slice `stat_rows`, after | **45 / 46 ms** | **49 / 46 ms** | **39 / 38 ms** | **38 / 42 ms** |
| site slice `patches`, before → after | 93 → 30 ms | 94 → 29 ms | 87 → 25 ms | 84 → 26 ms |
| oc1 + one patch `stat_rows`, before → after | 7.2 → 2.6 ms | 7.4 → 2.7 ms | 6.0 → 2.2 ms | 6.3 → 2.5 ms |
| oc1 `patches`, before → after | 76 → 4.7 ms | 77 → 4.8 ms | 63 → 3.9 ms | 65 → 4.1 ms |

  Every after-read returned the same rows as its before-read. Without the two-step sum, the site slice read 64–68 ms on 3.46.
- **Migration and rebuild cost** on the copy (this container): V0025 took 1.9 s on 3.46 and 1.4 s on 3.51. It wrote 138,102 rows (13.8 MiB, 35 % of `champion_stats`' 39.9 MiB) and put the slice index's 24.3 MiB on the free list. The rebuild's new `INSERT` takes 95–115 ms for one ladder with every patch (ADR-166 estimated +1.7 s for the whole copy). **On production** (≈ 475 MB of analytics tables, `champion_stats` a part of that), expect the migration to take seconds, not minutes, at the next release's first start. The new table should be 35–70 % of `champion_stats`: a smaller share where keys span many tiers, as on the copy, a larger one where most players sit in few tiers. Dropping the index frees pages of about the same order, so the file barely grows. The freed pages stay on the free list until a reclaim (design/07).
- **Rejected.** A covering index and summing over platform as well (ADR-166). Filling the totals from `match_facts`: a second pass over the facts, and two derivations that could drift. Folding the summed rows in Rust instead of SQL: unmeasured, and a larger change than the two-step sum.
- **Tests.** `src/db/tests.rs`: `v0025_sums_champion_stats_over_tier_and_drops_the_slice_index` (seeded at version 24: a key in three tiers, the other side, a remake, another patch and platform; hand-checked rows, `EXCEPT` both ways empty, `WITHOUT ROWID`, the index gone, `champion_stats` kept). V0020's test now expects the index gone. `src/archive/analytics/tests.rs`: `the_totals_are_the_stats_summed_over_tier`, `a_partial_rebuild_leaves_other_patches_totals_alone`, `tierless_and_tiered_reads_agree` (the summed row is the per-tier rows summed, across platform, patch, role, side, champion and remakes; the patch list's games are `champion_stats`'), `tierless_reads_read_the_totals` (with either table emptied, only its reads go blank), and `every_read_seeks_on_its_key` on twelve tables. `tests/dashboard.rs` and `tests/analytics.rs` write the totals beside the stats they write by hand, as a rebuild does.

## ADR-169 — Analytics responses cached per aggregate generation (2026-10-15)
Accepted (task PERF-03). design/04 §Cache tiers, §Analytics reads; design/11; ADR-099, ADR-166, ADR-168.
- **What.** The five analytics routes keep each 200's bytes and `ETag` in memory (`routes/analytics/cache.rs`), keyed by route, key scope, `championId` and every query parameter as parsed, with defaults applied. Two spellings of one query, or an unknown extra parameter, share an entry. A hit, 200 or 304, runs no `Db::read` and sends `X-Cache: HIT`; a computed response sends `MISS`; a 4xx or 5xx sends none and is never stored. The handlers are now parse → `serve` → `compute`, and `compute` is the code that ran before. Tags are byte-identical: the site's call tags `W/"r2GM8IHP…"` on the synthetic copy before and after.
- **Invalidation.**
  1. `aggregate:analytics` clears after its last commit and `record_run`, and **before** it publishes `analytics.updated`, so whatever acts on the event reads the new rows.
  2. A rebuild that fails also clears, because a step may already have committed.
  3. `clear()` bumps a generation under the lock that empties the map. A miss stores only if the generation it started at is still current, so a read that raced the commit can't store old numbers after the clear.
  4. Each entry records the Data Dragon version its names and tag were built at, and a lookup at another version is a miss. That covers every way the version moves, with no call from the sync.
  5. `/dev/reset` clears after its wipe. Nothing else writes the aggregates: the admin recompute queues the job, and the CLI has no writer.

  The job holds the same `Arc` as `AppState`. A hub subscriber couldn't order the clear before the warm.
- **Concurrency.** Misses share one computation through `SingleFlight`, keyed by `(generation, key)`, so a request after a clear never joins a read from before it. Ten concurrent cold requests for the site's call ran 5 reads, not 50.
- **The warm.** After each rebuild, for every queue in `LADDER_QUEUES`, with `{p}` the platform just rebuilt:
  - champions `queue&patch=all&minGames=0&limit=500` (the site);
  - champions `queue&minGames=0&limit=500` (the site's "newest", which sends no `patch`);
  - champions `platform={p}&queue&patch=all&limit=500` (the showcase home);
  - `patches?queue` (the site);
  - `patches?queue&platform={p}` (the showcase home).

  That is 5 entries and 18 reads per queue, 93 ms on the synthetic copy. Every queue is warmed because the clear drops them all. Back-to-back rebuilds each clear and re-warm, (N−1) × ~0.1–0.2 s wasted next to rebuilds of seconds to minutes. Champion pages aren't warmed; they fill on first view.
- **Size.** `ANALYTICS_CACHE_ENTRIES`, default 512, `0` stores nothing (every response `MISS`), at most 4096. The largest body is the champions list, one row per champion: 40,295 B for 171 rows, whatever `limit` says. So 512 entries is at most about 21 MB and 4096 about 170 MB. The LRU is a map and a use counter under a `std::sync::Mutex`, never held across an `.await`; a full map evicts the least recently used on insert. No new crate.
- **Visibility.** `X-Cache` feeds `proxy_requests_total{cache}` and the dashboard's recent requests by route, as for the other routes. There is no new metric. Analytics hits don't count in `proxy_cache_reads_total`, which is the Riot cache's hit ratio. No `X-Cache-Age` is sent: `computedAt` dates the body. A debug log line per miss gives its key and the `Db::read`s it ran; `Db::reads()` counts every read for the tests.
- **Measured** (release build, synthetic copy, 30 requests): the site's call, p50, 41.9 ms → **0.5 ms**; its 304, 41.2 → **0.4 ms**.
- **Rejected.**
  - A TTL: the data has none, it changes on commit.
  - Entries per consumer: the body doesn't depend on the consumer, and the key scope is the process's.
  - A byte cap: the bodies are bounded by the champion count.
  - `moka`: TinyLFU admission can refuse a new entry, and batched eviction isn't deterministic to test.
  - Clearing per ladder: every no-platform entry depends on every platform, so most entries go anyway.
  - Clearing on `patch.new`: `sync` sets the current version on every run, and a forced re-sync publishes without a new version.
- **Limits.** The clear is in-process. If `ROLE=api|worker` ever runs on a real shared store (Postgres is a stub, ADR-062), the clear must travel through the hub with a generation in the event.
- **Tests.** `tests/analytics.rs`:
  - `a_hit_runs_no_read_and_a_304_comes_from_memory`, `a_miss_is_read_once_then_served_from_memory_with_the_same_tag`;
  - `a_rebuild_clears_and_warms_what_sites_open_first`, `a_failed_rebuild_still_clears`;
  - `the_cap_evicts_the_least_recently_used`, `zero_keeps_nothing`;
  - `reordered_queries_and_other_consumers_share_an_entry`, `errors_are_not_cached`, `concurrent_misses_read_once`.

  Elsewhere: `tests/ddragon.rs` `a_new_patch_misses_the_analytics_cache`; `tests/dev_reset.rs` `a_reset_drops_the_analytics_responses`; unit tests in `cache.rs`, `analytics.rs`, `config/tests.rs` and `db/tests.rs`. Two existing tests that rewrite the tables in SQL now clear the cache after the write, as a rebuild would.

## ADR-170 — Tier ranges count each match once per floor (2026-10-15)
Accepted (owner request 2026-10-10, task FLT-03; owner answers 2026-10-15). design/04 §Schema, §Analytics reads; design/11 §Page map. Builds on ADR-123 and ADR-130; ADR-167/168/169 unchanged.
- **What.** `minTier=<TIER>` on `champions`, the detail, `matchups` and `builds` reads that tier and every one above it, in `riot::ladder::tiers()`' order. `UNKNOWN` is never in a range and isn't a floor.
  - The per-participant rows of the range's tiers are summed, which is exact because they are disjoint across tiers (FLT intro).
  - `Read.tier` is a `TierFilter`: `Any`, `One(tier)` or `AtOrAbove(floor)`. A read can't name a tier and a range at once.
  - A range is `tier IN (range ∩ the slice's tiers)` on `champion_stats` and on the facet and build tables. `stats_source` never sends a range to `champion_stats_totals`, which has no tier column.
- **Denominators: V0026.** `analytics_slices` and `champion_bans` count a match once in each tier it had a player in, so summed over a range they overcount. One Diamond player, three Master and two Unknown give a `DIAMOND+` pick rate of 0.5 instead of 1. Add a ten-Diamond match without the champion and summing gives 0.3333 instead of 0.5, while the ban rate comes out at 0.6667 instead of 0.5. So the error goes both ways; it is not the "above 1" the brief feared.
  - `analytics_floor_totals` and `champion_ban_floor_totals` count a match once in every floor at or below its highest stamped tier. Both are `WITHOUT ROWID` and keyed `(key_scope, platform, queue, floor_tier, patch[, champion_id], remake)`, with `floor_tier` stored as the tier's name: a read names one floor by equality and seeks through the patch.
  - A match whose players are all `UNKNOWN` or unstamped is in no floor, though it is in the V0015 totals.
  - `rebuild_champions` fills both in its transaction. A temp table holds each match's highest floor once; counts are taken per that tier and then spread over the floors below it.
  - The floor list is a `VALUES` built from `tiers()`, code constants like ADR-130's `Side`.
  - V0026 backfills every aggregated slice the same way, V0015's pattern: platform from the match id, only matches archived by the slice's `computed_at`. It was checked equal to a rebuild on a hand-built fixture, on the FLT-02 fixture and on two synthetic archives.
- **Rates.** The floor's totals go under `Rates`' `None` key when the read is a range, so `Rates::new` and `enrich` are unchanged. The summed row (`tier: null`) divides by its floor. Each `byTier` row, now only the range's tiers, divides by its own tier's slice.
- **`/patches` takes no range** (owner, 2026-10-15), as it takes no `tier` (ADR-130). Its games label the picker, and the champion page's patch list stays the champion's every game. Like any unknown parameter it is ignored, not refused.
- **Validation.** `minTier` is checked after `tier`, against Riot's tiers in Riot's casing. `tier` with `minTier` is a 400 `VALIDATION`, `querystring/minTier must NOT be sent with tier`, in ADR-130's house shape.
- **Bodies and ETags.**
  - Every body except `/patches` has `minTier` (`null` without one) beside `tier`, which is `null` under a range.
  - `minTier` is a part of every ETag and of the cache key (ADR-169), right after `tier`, so `tier=DIAMOND` and `minTier=DIAMOND` are separate entries.
  - The shape marker becomes `tier-side-range`. The extra part alone already moves every tag, but the marker records why, as ADR-130's did.
  - The warm list is unchanged: neither the site nor the showcase home sends a range.
- **Showcase.** The champion page's tier picker offers *Master+*, *Diamond+* and *Emerald+* above the single tiers, highest first (owner, 2026-10-15). `ch.tier = 'DIAMOND+'` is sent as `minTier=DIAMOND`, and the cards read `Diamond+ · Red side`. The header's tool row keeps its rows at 1280, 820 and 390 px, and the select its 113 px.
- **Measured** (this container; `docs/flt/FLT-03-plan.md` §9 has the scripts). `scripts/perf-synthetic.py`'s copy has no facts, so the rebuild and the backfill were timed on a facts-backed synthetic archive (seed 1, 10.7 KB bodies, players around a base tier from Emerald to Challenger, a quarter `UNKNOWN`).
  - **V0026:** 1.36 s at 200,000 facts and 3.70 s at 600,000; V0015's own backfill took 1.32 s on the same 200,000. Extrapolated to production's ~2.3 M facts, about 14 s here and 25–30 s at the next release's first start (V0025 ran about 1.6–2× slower there than here).
  - **Rebuild:** +4.7 s on the 36.2 s champion step (4 ladders, every patch, 600,000 facts), i.e. +13 %.
  - **Rows:** `champion_ban_floor_totals` is 9.6× `champion_ban_totals` (83,602 rows, 4.3 MiB) and `analytics_floor_totals` 10× `analytics_match_totals`. They are bounded by ladders × patches × 2 × 10 (× 171 champions), well under 1 % of production's 475 MB.
  - **Reads** on the PERF copy (SQLite 3.51, cold): the site slice at `minTier=EMERALD` is 121 ms, against 40 ms with no tier and 34 ms at `tier=EMERALD`. A range sums 5/6 of `champion_stats`, which the tierless read avoids. One champion's range read is 2–3 ms, and the floor reads match the V0015 reads (2.5 ms). Keeping the tier in the first sum's `GROUP BY` saved 190 → 121 ms.
- **Rejected.**
  - Summing the per-tier slices: wrong both ways, as above.
  - Floor totals worked out at read time from `match_facts`: design/04 keeps analytics reads off the facts.
  - Storing a rank instead of the tier's name: a read only ever looks up one floor by equality.
  - A `json_each` floor list: it would need a seventh parameter in `Scope::insert`.
  - One insert grouping per floor over facts × floors: 17.5 s instead of 4.7 s, because it sorts every match up to ten times.
  - A floor-summed stats table for range reads over the whole list: nothing sends one yet (finding PERF-05).
- **Tests.**
  - `src/archive/analytics/tests.rs`: `a_match_counts_once_in_each_floor_at_or_below_its_best_player`, `range_reads_sum_the_named_tiers`, `range_reads_never_read_the_stats_totals`, `a_partial_rebuild_leaves_other_patches_floor_rows_alone`, `the_floor_list_is_riots_tiers`. `every_read_seeks_on_its_key` adds a range read and the two floor tables, and checks that a range's first sum needs no sort.
  - `src/db/tests.rs`: `v0026_backfills_the_floors_of_every_aggregated_slice`.
  - `tests/analytics.rs`: `tier_ranges_count_a_match_once_on_every_route` (hand counts: Syndra 1/1, not 1/3; Nidalee at `CHALLENGER+` with remakes 1/1, where the every-tier total gives 0.5), `tier_and_min_tier_are_validated_on_every_route`, `a_range_and_a_tier_are_separate_entries`.
  - The OpenAPI and route snapshots; `tests/dom/showcase.test.mjs` (*Diamond+* sends `minTier=DIAMOND`).

## ADR-171 — Set builds against a lane opponent (2026-10-19)
Accepted (owner request, task FLT-04; plan approved 2026-10-19, size accepted the same day). design/04 §Schema, §Analytics reads; design/11 §Builds — notes for a frontend. Builds on ADR-101, ADR-120, ADR-121, ADR-129 and ADR-170. The plan, with its measurements, is `docs/flt/FLT-04-plan.md`.
- **What.** `GET …/champions/{id}/builds?opponentId=N` answers the champion's set builds in the games where it held a lane alone against `N`, also alone: `champion_matchups`' rule, so a shared lane and a mirror give none. Every build, with or without an opponent, gains `winRateLow`.
- **V0027: two tables.** `champion_matchup_builds` and `champion_matchup_build_parts`, `WITHOUT ROWID`, keyed `(…, patch, champion_id, role, opponent_id, remake, core[, part, value], tier, side)`: as `champion_matchups` up to the opponent and as `champion_builds` after it, tier and side last (ADR-129), so a read seeks through the remake. The spec listed tier and side before the patch; that would break ADR-129's seek. The parts are only `item3`, `boots`, `runes` and `spells`, to hold the rows down.
  - **Not backfilled.** These are per-participant rows, and re-deriving them in SQL would copy the rebuild into the migration (ADR-129's reason for V0017). Until a rebuild reaches a patch, the route answers the documented empty case. Production and the dev VM run `AGGREGATE_PATCH_LIMIT=0`, so their first rebuild fills every patch.
- **Rebuild.** In `rebuild_builds`' set-build transaction, after `champion_build_parts`. One temp table, `lane_builds`, holds each one-on-one laner joined to their `match_builds` row (two or more finished items, the same `key_scope`); both inserts read it (11.2 s against 19.0 s for restating the lane CTE twice, on the dev VM archive copy). The lane CTE is shared with `champion_matchups` as `lanes(extra)` and `one_on_one(join)`; the matchups keep their own narrower lane, since widening it cost them 30 %. The part values are one `PART_VALUE`, shared with `champion_build_parts`. Measured: +13 % on the whole rebuild.
- **Reads.** `Read.opponent_id`; `build_source(r)` returns the opponent tables and their `Keys` (`MATCHUP_BUILDS`, `opponent: true`) when it is set, else the set builds with `FACETS`, and is the only way to reach them. On those keys an open role is `role IN (the five lanes)`, so the seek reaches `opponent_id`. `top_lane` picks the lane with the most of those builds, ties to the lane that sorts first (ADR-121's second rule; every row is a lane). Tier, range and side need nothing new.
- **Route.** `Request::Builds(id, BuildsQuery { common, opponent_id })`. A query that has an `opponentId` validates `role` against the lanes, with the matchups route's message, before `opponentId` itself (`must be integer`, `must be >= 1`), in v1's property order. An opponent never met, or the champion itself, is 200 with `role: null`, `totalGames: 0`, `builds: []`: the true answer is "no games", so no 400. The body gains `opponentId` (`null` without one) and `opponentName` when Data Dragon names it.
- **`winRateLow`** is the lower bound of the 95 % Wilson score interval (z = 1.96), rounded as `winRate`; 0 for no games, as `rate()`. 7/10 gives 0.3968, 1/1 0.2065. It sits beside `rate()` so MU-01 can add it to the matchups with one call.
- **ETags and cache.** `opponentId` is a part of the builds tag after `championId`, and the last part of the builds cache key, so the other routes' keys don't move. The shape marker becomes `tier-side-range-opponent`. The warm list is unchanged.
- **Size: accepted** (owner, 2026-10-19). Counted on production on 2026-10-18: 4,019,071 laned build players and 3,008,892 `champion_matchup_builds` rows, about 1.3–1.45 GiB with the parts, against 475 MB of analytics tables before. The levers (two parts, a games floor, newest patches only) were offered and not taken.
- **Rejected.** `opponent_id` before `role` in the key (breaks the correspondence with `champion_matchups`; the `IN` over the lanes costs nothing measurable); a builds-only shape marker; a 400 for `opponentId == championId`; widening the matchups' lane CTE.
- **Showcase.** The *vs* picker and the matchup links are FLT-05 (plan §7). The route is listed in design/11's notes for a frontend.
- **Tests.**
  - `src/archive/analytics/tests.rs`: `matchup_builds_match_the_hand_computed_fixture`, `a_shared_lane_or_a_mirror_has_no_matchup_build`, `opponent_sums_never_exceed_the_set_builds_and_equal_them_in_one_on_one_games`, `opponent_reads_never_read_the_set_builds`, `the_top_lane_against_an_opponent_is_its_own`, `a_partial_rebuild_leaves_other_patches_matchup_builds_alone`; `every_read_seeks_on_its_key` and `range_reads_sum_the_named_tiers` cover the new tables.
  - `src/db/tests.rs`: `v0027_creates_the_matchup_build_tables`. `src/routes/analytics.rs`: `win_rate_low_is_the_wilson_lower_bound`, the cache key literals.
  - `tests/analytics.rs`: `builds_against_an_opponent`, `opponent_id_is_validated`, `an_unseen_opponent_or_a_mirror_is_an_empty_list`, `two_opponents_are_two_entries`; the builds and OpenAPI snapshots.

## ADR-172 — The *vs* picker on the champion page (2026-10-19)
Accepted (owner request, task FLT-05; plan approved 2026-10-19). design/11 §Page map, §Builds — notes for a frontend. Builds on ADR-121, ADR-125, ADR-130 and ADR-171. The plan, with its renders and measurements, is `docs/flt/FLT-05-plan.md`.
- **What.** The Builds card gains a picker, *vs any opponent* / *vs Galio · 9* / …, that sends `opponentId` to the builds route (ADR-171). Each Matchups row's *Lane* cell links to the builds against that opponent in that lane, sending `opponentId` and `role`.
- **The pick lives in the hash only**, `#/champion/{id}/builds/vs/{opponentId}[/{LANE}]`. Tier, side, patch, region and queue re-read the hash, so they keep the pick with no extra state, and Back steps through picks. Another champion's link and the search box carry no `vs`, so they drop it. `vs` must be a positive integer, else the hash is the plain `/builds` view; a lane that isn't one of `ROLES` is dropped and the route picks it. The picker marks its new hash as scrolled before assigning it, so picking doesn't move the page; a Matchups link is a new hash and brings the Builds card into view (SITE-10's once-per-hash rule).
- **No fallback against an opponent.** The detail route's per-item lists aren't against anyone, so against an opponent the card shows *No builds against X in this slice yet.* (or *yet*, *on patch N*) for an empty list, or the error for a failed call, even if the detail call failed too. The picker stays in both, so the user can step back without Back.
- **The picker's options** come from `vsChoices(matchups, picked, name)`: each opponent once, its games summed over lanes, most first, ties by name; a picked opponent the list lacks (another slice, a stale link) goes last without a count. Names fall back from Data Dragon to the routes' `opponentName`, then `#id`.
- **Filled in place.** When the matchups arrive, `loadMatchups` replaces the `<select>`'s options rather than re-rendering the card, so a focused picker keeps its focus and value (measured in Chromium; a re-render loses `document.activeElement`).
- **`SMALL_SAMPLE = 10`**, a shared constant MU-01 will reuse: a build under 10 games is dimmed (`opacity: .55`) and its title gains *fewer than 10 games*, with or without an opponent. Exactly 10 is not small.
- **Owner's answers (plan §9).** The picker leads the build-tab row (a second row at 1280 px in the narrow column, a third at 390 px with the longest name); dimming applies with or without an opponent; `winRateLow` is not shown on the page; the link target is the *Lane* cell, since the champion cell already links to the opponent's own page.
- **Rejected.** The picker in the card header (wrapped to 2–3 rows in FLT-04's plan); a separate link column in the Matchups table (fell off the right edge at 390 px); the pick in page state rather than the hash; moving the lane into the slice label (would change FLT-02/FLT-03's headers without an opponent).
- **Tests.** `tests/showcase.mjs`: `vs hashes and the vs picker choices (FLT-05)`. `tests/dom/showcase.test.mjs`: eight FLT-05 tests (the picker, the Lane link, the hash surviving each control, an unlisted opponent, a failed call, dimming, bad hashes, filling in place), and the harness's `hold`/`release()`. `tests/dom/showcase.browser.test.mjs`: the `champion vs` view at 1280, 820 and 390 px, and `Back after a matchup link restores the vs pick`. Ten mutations each fail one of them (plan, Implementation record).

## ADR-173 — The source `Dockerfile` builds on `main`, and on pull requests that can change it (2026-10-19)
Accepted (owner request "speed up CI and build time", task CI-01). Amends ADR-079.
- **Measured before** (runs 39149666697, 39149681337, 39143204943, 39142535874): `docker` took 5:52–8:11, the longest job on every pull request. 444 s of that was `docker/build-push-action` rebuilding the crate with fat LTO inside `rust:alpine`. The `type=gha` layer cache saves the dependency layer only, so the crate's own release build runs every time. `docker` is not a required check (ADR-079), but it decided when a PR's CI finished.
- **What.** On a pull request, the job diffs the merge commit against its first parent (the base branch; `fetch-depth: 2`) and pipes the names to `scripts/docker-paths.sh`. It builds only if one of these changed: `Dockerfile`, `.dockerignore`, `docker-compose.yml`, `Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `build.rs`, `rust-toolchain.toml` (the builder tag is bumped with it), `.github/workflows/ci.yml` or the script itself. Otherwise every step after the check is skipped and the job passes. A push to `main` always builds, and still writes the layer cache.
- **Why source edits don't count.** `build-musl` compiles the same source for the same musl target with the same release profile on every pull request. What only the `docker` job can catch is a broken Dockerfile, compose file, build context or toolchain image, and those are the listed paths. The one gap is a source file that newly reads something `.dockerignore` excludes (`include_str!` of a file under `/data`, say). The next push to `main` catches that, the way it caught everything the job missed before ADR-079 made it a separate job.
- **Rejected.** A third-party paths-filter action (one more dependency with write access to the job's outputs, for nine fixed names); `on.pull_request.paths` for the whole workflow (it would skip the required checks too); skipping the job outright with a job-level `if` (a skipped job has no steps to say why).
- **Tests.** `scripts/docker-paths.test.sh` in the `ops` job: each listed path builds, source and docs only skip, an empty diff skips, a final line without a newline still counts (a bug the test found), a path matches whole and not by suffix, and another workflow file skips.

## ADR-175 — The DOM and browser suites run in their own `ui` job (2026-10-19)
Accepted (owner request "speed up CI and build time", task CI-03). Amends ADR-074 and ADR-084, which put them at the end of `test`.
- **Measured before** (run 39149666697): in `test`, after the Rust suite, `npx playwright-core install --with-deps` took 18 s and `npm test` in `tests/dom` 56 s; with Node setup and the screenshot upload, about 80 s of the job's 5:34.
- **What.** A new `ui` job runs them: checkout, Node 22 with the npm cache, `npm ci`, the Chromium headless shell with its system packages, `npm test`, and the screenshot artefact, unchanged. The suites read `src/ui/*.html` straight from the checkout and start no server, so the job needs no Rust toolchain or cache. It runs beside `test` instead of after it.
- **The leaked-key grep stays in `test`.**
- **Required checks.** `ui` is a new job id. Until the owner adds it to branch protection, a failing DOM or browser suite no longer blocks a merge as it did inside `test`. The owner should add it (Settings → Branches → `main` → required status checks), keeping `test`.
- **Rejected.** Moving them into `acceptance`, which already sets up Node: that job is about 4 minutes, so it would become the slowest job.
